# UmerOS — Code Review Standards & Process

**Owner:** Code Review Expert (Kim)
**Status:** v1.43 — Adopted (Appendix: `tests/` + `Skills/Context File/` + `ai/` + `ui/` + `boot/` + `bin/` + `build/` + `cloud/` + `compatibility/` + `core/` + `dev/` + `drivers/` + `etc/` + `examples/` + `feedback/` + `fs/` + `home/` + `HostFiles/` + `initrd/` + `installer/` + `kernel/` + `legal/` + `lib/` + `liboqs/` + `media/` + `mnt/` + `network/` + `opt/` + `packages/` + `proc/` + `quantum/` + `root/` + `sbin/` + `scripts/` + `sdk/` study + `security/` study + `sources/` study + `srv/` study + `tmp/` study + `tools/` study + `usr/` study + `var/` study, 2026-08-22)
**Applies to:** All active Python code in the UmerOS repository
**Out of scope (do NOT review):** the `Old Linux Code/` reference tree (≈93k files of upstream Linux C), `node_modules/`, `*/__pycache__/`, and any generated `.pyz`/`.exe` build artifacts. These are reference/deps, not UmerOS source.
**Authoritative design sources:** (1) `MainTask/prompt/*.md` (Antigravity master prompt, engineering blueprint, model-specific prompts); (2) `MainTask/Raw Data/*.docx` (master design prompts); (3) **`Skills/Context File/umer_os_skills.json`** — the canonical agent-skill / design contract (tier labels, zero-trust, AI governance, per-file baseline, crypto standards, installer EULA, performance targets — see the study below). Where this doc and those sources disagree, the **design intent wins** and the code must be migrated — *except* where a source is itself aspirational (e.g. `README.md`'s Project Structure, H14), in which case the repo + `LICENSE`/`setup.py` win.

---

## 0. How to use this document

This is the single source of truth for how we review code in UmerOS. Use it two ways:

1. **Author self-check** — before opening a PR, walk Section 4 and confirm every applicable item is satisfied.
2. **Reviewer checklist** — during review, work top-to-bottom; every 🔴 must be resolved before approval, 🟡 should be resolved or explicitly waived by a maintainer, 💭 are discretionary.

The standard is grounded in (a) the **design intent** expressed across the `MainTask/prompt` and `MainTask/Raw Data` sources, and (b) the **actual state of the repo** (studied 2026-08-20): ~735 active Python modules, a zero-trust `security/` subsystem, 921 passing unittest tests, and an existing `.github/workflows/security_scan.yml`. Where the repo already has a good convention, we *codify* it. Where we found real gaps or design/code drift, we *enforce* them below.

---

## 1. Project context (so reviewers calibrate correctly)

**Vision (authoritative — quoted from the design sources, `MainTask/Raw Data/Umer OS Master Design Prompt chatgpt.docx` and the Antigravity master prompt):**
> Umer OS is envisioned as a **Python-first hybrid classical+quantum, AI-native, cross-device operating system** to eventually replace existing desktop and mobile OSs (Linux, Windows, Android, macOS, etc.). It blends a modern **microkernel architecture** with built-in **quantum simulation** and **future QPU integration**, **local AI assistants**, and full backward compatibility. Key goals include **minimal storage/memory footprint**, **maximal efficiency** (through AI scheduling and quantum-inspired algorithms), and an **intuitive, adaptive UI/UX**. Umer OS will support a universal developer ecosystem (SDK, package manager, container system) and seamless cross-device/cloud sync. The system will be **modular**, **secure (zero-trust, quantum-safe)**, and **data-privacy-first**.

Every checklist item and hotspot below is measured against this vision.

| Fact | Value (from design sources + code) | Review implication |
|---|---|---|
| Language | **Backend = Python ≥ 3.12** for ~95%+ of logic (OS, services, AI, drivers, kernel glue). C/ASM only for bootloader entry, HW interrupt table, context-switch stub — always behind a `ctypes` binding. **No other backend language is permitted.** Frontend language is contested — see §1.1 and the UI row (H11) | Flag any new backend module not in Python; do not use removed stdlib (`imp`) |
| License | **GPL-3.0** is canonical — confirmed by `LICENSE`, `setup.py`, **and `README.md`** (GPLv3 badge + License section). The Antigravity master prompt, engineering blueprint, and `docs/developer_guide.md` still say **GPL-3.0 (GNU General Public License Version 3)** | Decision made: GPL-3.0 wins (3 of 4 sources). 🟡 Fix the Apache-2.0 strays (Hotspot H7) so docs/prompts stop contradicting the license |
| Nature | **Research prototype** that runs **atop an existing kernel (Linux userspace)** or a minimal bootloader — *not* bare-metal production | Be realistic about claims; no "magic" performance; simulation is fine, mislabeling it as real is not |
| Domain | Microkernel + AI-native + quantum-inspired OS | Security & correctness carry higher weight than polish |
| Tests | `tests/` tree (**39 files** + `tests/quantum/`). **Framework split:** top-level `test_*.py` + `run_initrd_tests.py` / `run_lib_tests.py` / `run_root_tests.py` use `unittest`; `tests/quantum/` uses **pytest** (`pytest.fixture` + bare `assert`). No root `pytest.ini` / `pyproject` / `setup.cfg` / `tox.ini`. **No CI step runs the suite** (only `security_scan.yml` exists); the 3 `run_*.py` scripts cover only **4 of 39** modules | New behavior needs tests — but first wire ONE discoverable runner + a CI test step (H16) so "green" is enforceable |
| Security model | **Zero-trust**: every process verified at creation, every IPC message signed (HMAC-SHA3/SHA256), every network call validated; capability + namespace isolation; PQC (Kyber/Dilithium) | Any code that bypasses trust gets the hardest scrutiny |
| UI | **Flutter (Dart) is canonical** (decided 2026-08-20). Canonical source: **`ui/flutter_ui/`** — a real Flutter project (`lib/main.dart`, 18 app screens, `pubspec.yaml`, `analysis_options.yaml`, `test/`). Legacy frontend: **`ui/*.py`** (`fluidic_ui.py` CLI shell, `umer_de.py` Tkinter DE, `theme.py`, `launch_gui.py`, `umeros_gui.py`) + Kivy references in `README.md`/`setup.py` — all **legacy drift to migrate** (see §1.1/H15/H25). "Flutter with java" was wrong: Flutter = Dart; Java/Kotlin only for native Android platform channels | **Decision: Flutter canonical.** New UI only in `ui/flutter_ui/` (Dart); retire/migrate `ui/*.py`; drop `kivy` from `requirements.txt` (H15/H25) |
| Dependencies | `requirements.txt` declares ~32 active deps + 4 commented dev tools (black/flake8/mypy/pylint). Most float (`>=`); `fastapi>0.110.0` / `uvicorn[standard]>0.30.0` / `httpx>0.27.0` are unbounded `>`; `g4f>=0.4.0` (free GPT-4o, no key) is a supply-chain/ToS risk | 🟡 Pin + hash; review `g4f` before reliance (H15) |
| Docs accuracy | `README.md` "Project Structure" lists phantom modules (`kernel/ipc.py`, `kernel/hal.py`, `quantum/simulator.py`, `security/crypto.py`, `fs/quantum_fs.py`…) and omits real heavy dirs (`bin/`, `etc/`, `lib/`, `usr/`, `initrd/`, `drivers/` ~77 files). Code examples import those phantoms; stats claim ~3,500 LOC / 12 modules vs actual ~735 active modules | ⚠️ Drift (H14): README is aspirational, not a source of truth |
| CI | Bandit + Safety + Trivy + OWASP ZAP on push/PR to `main` | Extend, don't replace (see Section 7) |

**Source-of-truth reconciliation (decided 2026-08-20):** where README/design docs and the repo disagree, **the repo + `LICENSE`/`setup.py` win**. Two past inconsistencies are now *resolved in direction*: **H7 → GPL-3.0 is canonical** (LICENSE + setup.py + README all say GPLv3; fix the Apache-2.0 strays in `developer_guide.md` and the master prompts); **H11 → Flutter (Dart) is canonical** (decided 2026-08-20, overriding the earlier Kivy call; remove/refresh Kivy from `requirements.txt`/README/`setup.py`). A brand-new drift, **H14**, captures that `README.md` documents a *different, aspirational* project layout and broken code examples — do not block real PRs on README's stated structure.

### 1.1 Language & tech-stack policy (decided 2026-08-20)

Enforced per the user's directive *"write code only in Python … front end in Flutter"* — **now DECIDED (Flutter wins over the prior Kivy call):**

- **Backend = Python only.** All OS logic, services, AI, drivers, and kernel glue are **Python ≥ 3.12**. No other backend language is permitted (C/ASM only for the bootloader entry / HW interrupt table / context-switch stub, behind a `ctypes` binding). Flag any new backend module not in Python.
- **Frontend = Flutter (Dart) — DECIDED.** This overrides the earlier *Kivy canonical* decision (H11). Flutter UIs are written in **Dart**, not Java — correct the instruction's wording. Java (or Kotlin) is used only for *native platform-channel* code on Android, never the app logic. The repo currently has **no Flutter/Dart source** and is Kivy-based in `README.md`/`setup.py`, so this is a **migration**: drop `kivy` from `requirements.txt` (H15), plan the Flutter frontend, and treat existing Kivy references as legacy drift to refresh (like H14).
- **Reviewer action:** new backend code must be Python; the **canonical frontend lives in `ui/flutter_ui/` (Dart)** — all new UX work goes there. Any `*.java` in the frontend is flagged unless it is native Android platform-channel code; flag any *new* Python/Tkinter/Kivy UI in `ui/*.py` as legacy (retire/migrate to Flutter, H25); and **every frontend PR must satisfy the HCI checklist (§4.8)** — Human-Computer Interaction principles are a frontend design mandate.

**Tier-labeling is MANDATORY (design rule, non-negotiable).** Every module/feature is labelled `[TODAY]` ✅ / `[EXPERIMENTAL]` 🔬 / `[FUTURE]` 🔮. *Unlabelled features are rejected at review.* Consequence: a PR that adds behavior without a tier label is a 🔴.

**Command-interface contract (dominant pattern — enforce consistently):**
Every shell command implements `execute(self, args=None) -> int` (returns a POSIX-style exit code), exposes `self.name`, `self.description`, `self.usage`, supports `--help`/`--version`, and ships a `_selftest()`. The base class `core/command.py` currently declares `execute(self, *args) -> Any` with `name/description/category/privileges` — **known mismatch** (Hotspot H6). New code follows the `args=None -> int` convention; converge `core/command.py` to it.

---

## 2. Principles

1. **Review teaches, it doesn't gatekeep.** Every comment explains *why* and offers a concrete fix.
2. **Trust the zero-trust model.** UmerOS assumes no process is trusted. PRs that execute untrusted input, shell out to the host, or weaken capability checks get the hardest scrutiny.
3. **Microkernel boundaries are contracts.** Kernel core handles *only* IPC, scheduling, memory mapping, capability enforcement. Everything else is a user-space Python service; a crash there must not panic the kernel. Review for privilege creep.
4. **Small, focused PRs review better.** Aim for one logical change per PR; keep diffs reviewable (<~400 lines ideal).
5. **Automate the boring parts.** Formatting, linting, static security, and type-checking run in CI so humans focus on logic and design.
6. **Tests are part of the change, not an add-on.** No test = no merge for anything behavioral.
7. **Green is not optional.** The full suite (currently 921) must pass with zero regressions; new modules add their own `_selftest()` + suite tests.
8. **No impossible claims.** Code/comments must not assert capabilities the hardware can't deliver. Use "simulated" / "quantum-inspired" / "EXPERIMENTAL" where no QPU/NPU exists. "Zero-error qubits" is FUTURE-only.

---

## 3. Severity model

| Marker | Meaning | Merge impact |
|---|---|---|
| 🔴 **Blocker** | Correctness bug, security vulnerability, data loss, broken contract, missing critical error handling, or **violation of a design mandate** (tier label, zero-trust, legal/privacy) | **Must be fixed** before approval |
| 🟡 **Suggestion** | Real improvement: missing validation, confusing logic, missing test, perf issue, duplication, convention drift | Fix or get explicit maintainer waiver |
| 💭 **Nit** | Style/naming/doc nicety; low risk | Author's discretion |

---

## 4. The review checklist

Work this list for every PR. Items marked **[UmerOS]** are project-specific and derived from the design sources and the codebase study.

### 4.1 Correctness 🔴/🟡
- [ ] **Does it do what the PR claims?** Read the description, then verify the code path actually implements it.
- [ ] **Return contracts honored.** Commands return `int` (exit code); async kernel APIs are `await`ed; `MemoryManager.free` raises on double-free; `CapabilityManager.query` never raises. Flag deviations.
- [ ] **No silent fallthrough.** `execute()` handles `--help`/`--version` and returns non-zero on bad/missing args (per `test_bin.py` expectations).
- [ ] **Off-by-one / boundary checks** on indexes, slices, qubit counts, memory pages (page-aligned to 4096).
- [ ] **[UmerOS] Quantum qubit sizing** — never hardcode qubit counts; use `_required_qubits(circuit_ops)` (past critical bug).
- [ ] **[UmerOS] Stale exports** — `__init__.py` files re-export only symbols that exist (past break: `MicrocodeVendor` vs `CPUVendor`). Grep imports vs definitions.
- [ ] **[UmerOS] Microkernel boundary** — no filesystem/network/driver logic sneaks into the privileged core; new work belongs in a user-space service.

### 4.2 Security 🔴 (highest weight for this project)
- [ ] **No `eval`/`exec` on untrusted input.** 🔴 `initrd/ai_helper.py:166` does `eval(line)` on a history file (marked `# noqa: S307`); must-fix — parse instead of eval, or gate behind a capability + signed history.
- [ ] **No command injection via `subprocess`.** 🔴 `bin/user_commands.py:_exec_command` runs `[sh, "-c", command]` as another user — confirm `command` is not attacker-controlled and `shell=False` semantics hold. 🟡 `bin/boolean_ops.py:411` and `etc/issue_motd.py` shell out to the host; prefer arg lists (no `shell=True`).
- [ ] **No hardcoded secrets.** 🔴 `lib/security.py:72` `PASSWORD = "password"` default; 🟡 `scripts/test_endpoint.py` `secret="test-secret"`, `usr/misc_data_manager.py` `BIRTHTOKEN="birthtoken"`. Use `os.urandom(32)` / env vars / secret store.
- [ ] **[UmerOS] Exposed credential remediation.** 🔴 `settings.local.json` contains a **live OpenRouter key** (`sk-or-v1-…`). Revoke+rotate it, add to `.gitignore`, and purge from git history before any merge touches it.
- [ ] **[UmerOS] Zero-trust enforcement (design mandate).** New syscalls/IPC/fs/network ops must go through `CapabilityManager`/`SecuritySandbox`; IPC messages HMAC-signed & replay-protected; every process verified at creation (code-signing). Bypassing = 🔴.
- [ ] **[UmerOS] Crypto standards (design mandate).** PQC via `liboqs` (Kyber/Kyber ML-KEM, Dilithium ML-DSA); hashing SHA3-512; symmetric AES-256-GCM; TLS post-quantum 1.3. No home-grown crypto.
- [ ] **[UmerOS] AI self-healing is gated.** The design allows the AI to generate & deploy hot-patches and roll back on regression. Any such path is 🔴-review: generated code must be **capability-scoped, sandbox-executed, logged to an audit trail, and rollback-tested** before auto-apply; never apply untrusted LLM output to a privileged path.
- [ ] **[UmerOS] Self-healing patch is never executed.** `umer_ai.py:SelfHealingEngine.generate_patch` returns *code strings* (TODAY: comment-only stubs — safe). Any FUTURE path that `exec`/`eval`s a generated patch must satisfy the H12 gate (capability scope + sandbox + audit + rollback). The standalone `ai/self_healing.py:SelfHealingService.mitigate` "restarts process in isolated state" with **no sandbox/audit/rollback** — must be brought under the H12 gate before any auto-recovery (Hotspot H21).
- [ ] **[UmerOS] Package integrity.** `umer-pkg` artifacts (`.umerpkg`) must be cryptographically signed; installs verify signatures (chain-of-trust). Review any change to the package/签名 flow as security-critical.
- [ ] **[UmerOS] Installer legal mandate.** Every installer action must be preceded by the EULA/"I AGREE" check (design non-negotiable). PRs touching `installer/` must preserve this; no silent install.
- [ ] **[UmerOS] Boot-path integrity is fail-closed.** The kernel hash check (`boot/bootloader.py:verify_kernel`) and EFI binary trust (`boot/efi_system.py:is_binary_trusted`) must **reject** unknown/missing/forbidden binaries — never return `True` permissively (Hotspots H27/H28, extends H17). The "I AGREE" liability waiver must not be auto-accepted (H29). — ✅ base class **H17** (`security/security.py` `SecureBoot.verify_image`/`verify_bytes`) remediated 2026-08-24 to fail-closed: default `strict_mode=True`, unknown component denied in both strict (raises) and dev (returns `False`) modes; see `# [FIX H17]`.
- [ ] **[UmerOS] OTA/update integrity is fail-closed.** The over-the-air update path (`cloud/ota_updater/update_system.py`) must **verify the publisher's signature before applying** any payload — never apply an unsigned/unverified update. `verify_and_apply` currently returns `True` and applies even when no crypto engine is present (and, when present, self-signs the payload instead of verifying the manifest signature) — a fail-open supply-chain gap (Hotspot H46, same class as H17/H27/H28, breaks the signed-`.umerpkg` mandate, §4.2). Any missing/invalid signature must **abort** the update; wire it to the real `CryptoEngine`/`Dilithium` `verify()` path in `security/`.
- [ ] **[UmerOS] Compatibility-launched foreign binaries are sandboxed + capability-gated.** `compatibility/container_engine.py` launches Linux ELF / Windows `.exe` (Wine) / Android APK (ADB) via `subprocess.Popen` (arg-list, **no `shell=True` — good**) but **unsandboxed** (no chroot/namespace/resource quotas) and inherits the full env; `compatibility/container.py:ZeroTrustContainer.execute_binary` calls `capabilities.check(...)` but only *prints* a message and still runs the binary — a **fail-open** zero-trust gate (Hotspot H51, same class as H17/H27/H28/H46). Before promotion to `[TODAY]`, wrap every launch in an isolated namespace + drop capabilities + gate under `CapabilityManager`; **never ignore a failed capability check**. — ✅ **H51** remediated 2026-08-24: `ZeroTrustContainer.execute_binary` now gates execution on `capabilities.query(container_id, "HARDWARE")` and returns `False` (deny) when the capability is absent instead of running unconditionally; see `# [FIX H51]`. The broader namespace-isolation/resource-quota TODO remains unaddressed.
- [ ] **[UmerOS] Auth secrets are never static / fallback is fail-closed.** Any service that verifies tokens must use a configured IdP/key and **must refuse to serve** when that key is missing — never fall back to a hardcoded static secret. `drivers/driver_service.py` (`/proc` emulation API) currently falls back to a **hardcoded HS256 secret `"test-secret"`** when OIDC env vars are unset and then serves every endpoint (incl. `/pid/{pid}/environ`, which leaks process environment/secrets, and an unauthenticated `/metrics`) — a weak-default/static-secret auth gap (Hotspot H64, same family as H17/H27/H28/H46/H51). Gate sensitive `/proc` entries (environ, fd, cmdline, metrics) behind owner/ptrace-style checks; in non-dev deployments require OIDC + fail-closed.
- [ ] **Deserialization.** No `pickle.loads`/`yaml.load`/`marshal` on untrusted bytes (keep it that way).
- [ ] **Bandit clean.** New code must not introduce Bandit findings above the agreed baseline (Section 7).

### 4.3 Privacy & AI governance 🔴/🟡
- [ ] **[UmerOS] Opt-in only.** AI training / cloud offload / telemetry require explicit per-session user consent; no personal data leaves the device without permission (design mandate). Flag any default-on data egress.
- [ ] **[UmerOS] Consent & erasure.** Consent logged; `right to erasure` (`umer-privacy clear-all`) supported; anonymise before any sync (GDPR/ISO alignment).
- [ ] **[UmerOS] Local-first.** Inference runs on-device; cloud delegation explicit + consented. Review network calls from `ai/` for silent exfiltration.
- [ ] **[UmerOS] AI egress consent (🔴).** `ai/umer_ai.py:LocalAIAssistant.query` must call `AIGovernance.check_consent("online_ai")` (or equivalent, logged) **before** delegating to `OnlineProvider` (which POSTs the user prompt to an external API endpoint). The module's own privacy guarantee — *"NO user data leaves the device by default"* — is broken if prompts leave without explicit, recorded consent. Flag any `ai/` path that sends user text off-device without a consent gate (Hotspot H18).

### 4.4 Maintainability 🟡/💭
- [ ] **[UmerOS] Tier label present** on the module (`[TODAY]`/`[EXPERIMENTAL]`/`[FUTURE]`) and on each non-obvious feature (design rule: unlabelled = rejected).
- [ ] **[UmerOS] `boot/` tier labels (H31):** only `bootloader.py` carries a `[TODAY]` label; add one to every other `boot/` module. `boot/init.py` also skips the per-file baseline (no `from __future__ import annotations`, no `logging`, no docstrings) and must be brought up to standard (H32).
- [ ] **[UmerOS] Per-file baseline (design mandate):** every file includes `try/except` error handling, `logging` calls, **Python type hints**, and **Google-style docstrings**. Missing any = 🟡.
- [ ] **Public functions have type hints.** `from __future__ import annotations` at top.
- [ ] **[UmerOS] Command-interface consistency** — commands match `execute(args=None)->int` (POSIX exit code) + `self.name/description/usage` + `_selftest()` (see H6). **Root cause:** the base class `core/command.py:30` still declares `execute(self, *args) -> Any`, which is why 27/44 `bin/` modules inherit the wrong signature (H35/H55) — fix the base class first, then the subclasses. Also enforce the declared `privileges` in the base `execute()` (Hotspot H56); do not let `su`/`login`/root commands run without a capability check (§4.2).
- [ ] **Specific exceptions, not bare `except`.** 🟡 ~30+ broad `except Exception`/`except:` in `bin/` mask real errors; catch the specific type and log.
- [ ] **Naming & clarity.** Classes `CapWords`, functions `snake_case`, constants `UPPER`. Self-documenting names over comments.
- [ ] **No dead/commented code** left in (e.g. the commented `exec(chat_code, …)` in `kernel/umer_kernel.py:1717` — track it in an issue, don't leave it inline).
- [ ] **Duplication** extracted into shared helpers (esp. repeated FHS path logic across `bin/`/`usr/`/`lib/`).
- [ ] **[UmerOS] Logging vs print** — error/diagnostic output uses `loguru`/`logging`, not `print`, except interactive shell stdout where print is acceptable.
- [ ] **[UmerOS] Realism labels** — comments don't claim QPU/fault-tolerant behavior that doesn't exist; mark `# TODO: QPU integration` on future hooks.
- [ ] **[UmerOS] `ai/` per-file baseline + tier labels.** `ai/assistant.py` and `ai/self_healing.py` currently violate the baseline (no type hints, no docstrings, no `logging`, **no tier label**, use `print()`). `ai/providers.py` and `ai/resource_predictor.py` lack `[TODAY]/[EXPERIMENTAL]/[FUTURE]` labels on their classes. Every `ai/` module must carry tier labels + the full baseline before approval (Hotspot H19).
- [ ] **[UmerOS] No duplicated AI stacks.** `ai/` ships parallel implementations: `assistant.py:AIAssistant` ≈ `umer_ai.py:LocalAIAssistant`; `self_healing.py:SelfHealingService` ≈ `umer_ai.py:SelfHealingEngine`; `resource_predictor.py:ResourcePredictor` ≈ `umer_ai.py:AIResourceManager` (both EWMA + z-score spike detection, *different* `SPIKE_Z_THRESHOLD`: 2.0 vs 2.5). Consolidate into `umer_ai.py`; delete or clearly mark the standalone stubs `EXPERIMENTAL`/legacy (Hotspot H19).
- [ ] **[UmerOS] `bin/` study notes (v1.7).** `bin/` = ~44 FHS `/bin` + `/usr/*` command modules (≈19k LOC). (a) **0/44** carry a tier label — every command file violates the mandatory label rule (H40); (b) **27/44** use the wrong `def execute(self, *args)` signature instead of `def execute(self, args=None) -> int` (H35/H6) — only 12 modules use the correct form; (c) `su` (`user_commands.py`) spawns `[sh,"-c",command]` **as another user** and its `_exec_shell` is a stub that `return 0` (H36/H4); (d) `LoginCommand` `-f`/`-F` sets `skip_auth=True`, bypassing password auth (H37 🔴); (e) `ChageCommand` is defined twice (`usr_cmds.py:3045`, `usr_commands.py:1938`) (H38); (f) ~30+ broad `except` mask real errors (H8/H39). Subprocess usage is otherwise safe: arg-list + `timeout`, no `shell=True`, and no `eval`/`os.system`/`pickle`/`yaml.load` anywhere in `bin/`.
- [ ] **[UmerOS] `dev/` study notes (v1.12).** `dev/` = ~43 FHS `/dev` **virtual device-node filesystem** modules (≈6,800 LOC): a central `DeviceManager`/`DeviceNode`/`DeviceType` (`dev/core.py`) registry plus ~40 pseudo-device / subsystem / command modules (null/zero/random/full/tty/ptmx, input, shm, net/tun, fuse, memory `/dev/mem`+`/dev/kmem`+`/dev/port`, SCSI, NVMe, KVM, dri, snd, and the `udevadm`/`losetup`/`makedev`/`mknod` commands). Overall the **cleanest folder security-wise**: **no** `eval`/`exec`/`os.system`/`subprocess`/`shell=True`/`pickle`/`yaml.load`/`ctypes` anywhere, and licensing is **clean GPLv3** (headers say `Licence: GPLv3`; zero Apache-2.0 strays — a good counterexample to `boot/`). But: (a) 💭 **0/43** files carry a tier label (H58 — violates §4.4); (b) 🟡 the base `DeviceNode` defaults to `mode=0o666` (world-writable) and a long list of nodes are explicitly `0o666` (`null`/`zero`/`full`/`tty`/`ptmx`/`shm`/`tun`/`fuse`/`i2c`/misc-char/misc-system…) — only the security-sensitive memory devices correctly use `0o640` (H59); (c) 🟡 `DeviceManager.sync_to_filesystem()` (`dev/core.py:211`) calls `os.mknod`/`os.mkfifo`/`os.symlink_to` to materialize **real** device nodes into the VFS **without a capability/privilege gate** — on a real `/dev` with host privileges this creates device special files (incl. world-writable ones from the `0o666` default), a zero-trust gap (H60, same "privileged op / no gate" family as H27/H28/H46/H51); (d) 💭 the `DeviceManager` singleton registry (`self._nodes`) has **no lock** — concurrent registration/hotplug could race (H61). `memory_devices.py` is the model to copy: `STRICT_DEVMEM` restricts `/dev/mem` to `ALLOWED_RANGES`, `/dev/kmem` is `AVAILABLE=False` (returns zeros), and access checks log warnings before denying.
- [ ] **[UmerOS] `drivers/` study notes (v1.13).** `drivers/` = **75 Python modules (~34,853 LOC)** — the **kernel driver subsystem** (bus/device/driver framework: `device.py`/`bus.py`/`platform_driver.py`/`device_registry.py` + ~70 subsystem drivers: irq, usb, crypto, pci, input, mtd, dmabuf, tty, i2c, gpio, cpuidle, device_io, clock, thermal, reset, spi, iio, pinctrl, console, edac, nvmem, regulator, nfc, mailbox, nvme, rtc, devfreq, pwm, dmaengine, scsi, ata, counter, acpi, virtio, soundwire, tee, phy, ntb, remoteproc, fpga, pwrseq, rpmsg, framebuffer, interconnect, led, extcon, rfkill, backlight, `pci_endpoint`, `example_*`/`hello_char_driver`). It is a large **simulation of the Linux driver model** (no real HW access). **Clean:** **no** `eval`/`exec`/`os.system`/`subprocess`/`shell=True`/`pickle`/`yaml.load` anywhere; `crypto.py` uses `os.urandom` (CSPRNG) for every nonce/key (good RNG hygiene); `device_io.py` correctly locks its registries (`_lock`), claims regions (duplicate-key guard), tracks owner, and protects read-only ports. **But mostly off-baseline and zero-trust-gapped:** (a) 💭 **0/75** files carry a tier label (H62 — worst raw count yet); (b) 🟡 **0/75** carry a GPL license header (H63 — should be GPLv3 per H7; unlike `dev/`, which had clean GPLv3, `drivers/` has none at all); (c) 🔴 `driver_service.py` falls back to a **hardcoded static JWT secret `"test-secret"`** (HS256) when OIDC env vars are unset and serves every `/proc` endpoint — incl. `/pid/{pid}/environ` (process env / secrets) and an **unauthenticated `/metrics`** — a weak-default/static-secret auth gap in the same family as H17/H27/H28/H46/H51 (H64); (d) 🟡 the entire driver subsystem has **no capability gating** — MMIO/port I/O/DMA (`device_io.py`), PCI region claim (`pci.py`), and crypto key-gen/RNG (`crypto.py`) are callable by any in-process code with no `CapabilityManager` check; only `driver_service.py` imports from `security` (and only `tls_utils`) (H66); (e) 💭 `ctypes` is imported but never called in `device_io.py` + `irq.py` (dead import; if ever wired to real HW it becomes a privileged surface — keep `[EXPERIMENTAL]` + gate it) (H65); (f) 💭 `crypto.py` registers weak algos (DES, MD5) and implements ciphers in pure Python (not constant-time → side-channel risk) (H67); (g) 💭 `device_registry.py` + other driver registries mutate a global dict with **no lock** (like `dev/` H61; `device_io.py` is the locked counterexample) (H68); (h) 🟡 **19/75** files lack `from __future__ import annotations` (incl. `driver_service.py`, `device.py`, `bus.py`, `platform_driver.py`, all `example_*`) and `driver_service.py` uses `loguru` instead of the mandated `logging` (H8 baseline skip) (H69).

- [ ] **[UmerOS] `etc/` study notes (v1.14).** `etc/` = **81 FHS `/etc` configuration-manager modules (~24,419 LOC)**, flat (no subdirs) — managers for PAM (`pam_config.py` 1821 LOC), fstab/crypttab/tmpfiles/udev/sysconfig, init, login, hostname, locale/timezone, modules, cron, inet services, issue/motd, sudoers, critical-files, and 60+ more. **Subprocess discipline is good:** `subprocess` in 34 files, **all arg-list, `shell=True` = 0**, and no `eval`/`exec`/`os.system`/`pickle`/`yaml.load`/`ctypes` anywhere (injection-safe). Positive examples: `critical_files.py` protects critical files with `os.chmod 0o440`/`0o644`; `fstab_manager.py` mounts tmpfs with secure defaults (`noexec,nodev,nosuid`) and uses arg-list `df` calls. **But mostly off-baseline + zero-trust-gapped:** (a) 💭 **0/81** files carry a tier label (H70 — largest raw count, 81 files, worse than `drivers/` 75 / `bin/` 44 / `dev/` 43); (b) 🟡 **license inconsistency**: 28/81 GPLv3/GPL-3.0 (✓ canonical H7), **6/81 Apache-2.0 strays** (`__init__.py`, `config_manager.py`, `critical_files.py`, `network_config.py`, `passwd_group.py`, `shell_config.py` — violates H7), and **47/81 have NO license header at all** — including the biggest modules `pam_config.py` (1821), `module_config.py` (1395), `locale_timezone.py` (1383), `sysconfig.py` (1335), `udev_rules.py` (1209), `fstab_manager.py` (1185), `tmpfiles.py` (1151), `sudoers.py` — so the majority of `etc/` ships unlicensed (H71, extends H63/H30 and corrects the earlier "0 missing" note); (c) 🟡 `pam_config.py` has a weak-auth **detector** (`_WEAK_PATTERNS` flags `pam_permit.so`/`nullok`) but `_validate_entries` only *appends warnings* — it does **NOT reject** the write, so `add_auth_rule` still persists a `pam_permit`/`nullok` rule to `/etc/pam.d/*` (detect-but-don't-fail-closed, same family as H17/H27/H28/H46/H51/H64) (H72); (d) 🟡 `etc/` config managers write **real privileged `/etc` paths with no capability gate** — `sudoers.py` (`SudoersManager`) writes `/etc/sudoers` + `/etc/sudoers.d/*` (incl. `NOPASSWD:` privilege-escalation rules) with no `CapabilityManager` check and no validation that a rule doesn't grant blanket `NOPASSWD: ALL`; the entire subsystem is un-gated on privileged config writes (only `cups_config.py` mentions "capability" — a false positive, it's the printer *capability database*) — same "privileged op / no gate" family as H27/H28/H46/H51/H60/H66 (H73). `issue_motd.py` host-shell-out is already tracked (H5/§4.2) and is arg-list-safe.

- [ ] **[UmerOS] `examples/` study notes (v1.15).** `examples/` = **2 tiny illustrative modules (62 LOC total)**: `run_demo.py` (47 LOC — a `demo()` that wires the real `kernel.scheduler`/`kernel.ipc`/`kernel.hal`/`quantum.simulator` together and exercises `HAL.init_device`/`HAL.read_register` + `IPCBroker.register` + a `SimpleQuantumSimulator` job + the scheduler) and `tasks.py` (15 LOC — two cooperative `asyncio` example tasks). **Security-clean:** no `subprocess`/`shell=True`/`eval`/`exec`/`os.system`/`pickle`/`yaml.load`/`ctypes`, no hardcoded secrets, no network calls. **But it models the wrong baseline and the wrong trust posture for a zero-trust OS:** (a) 💭 **0/2** files carry a tier label (H74 — same gap as every other folder); (b) 💭 **0/2** carry a license header (should be GPLv3 per H7); (c) 💭 the per-file baseline is skipped — no `from __future__ import annotations`, `print` instead of `logging`, and the example functions (`demo`/`simple_io_task`/`long_compute_task`) have **no Google docstrings**; (d) 💭 `run_demo.py` exercises **privileged surfaces** (`HAL.init_device`/`HAL.read_register` HW access, `IPCBroker` IPC) **with no `CapabilityManager` check and no HMAC-signed IPC** (H75) — fine for a throwaway demo, but `examples/` is the first code new contributors copy, so it should model the zero-trust mandate (capability-gated privileged ops + signed IPC, §4.2) rather than the open one. Treat it as the canonical "starter template" and bring it up to the baseline.

- [ ] **[UmerOS] `feedback/` study notes (v1.16).** `feedback/` = **2 files (185 LOC)** — a community-feedback subsystem **stub**: `models.py` (125 LOC — `FeedbackEntry`/`FeedbackKind`/`FeedbackStatus`/`FeedbackPriority` dataclasses + `enum`s, clean: `from __future__`, type hints, docstrings, `uuid.uuid4()` IDs, no dangerous calls) and `__init__.py` (60 LOC — package init). **The package is currently BROKEN and non-functional:** `__init__.py` does `from collector/tracker/channels/gfdl/manager import ...` but **none of those 5 modules exist anywhere in the repo** (only `models.py` + `__init__.py` are present), so `import feedback` raises `ModuleNotFoundError` at import time; the docstring advertises a full collector/tracker/channels/gfdl/manager/cli framework that is entirely unimplemented (H76 — import-blocking defect; cf. H14 aspirational drift, but this is real broken code). Also: (a) 🟡 **license inconsistency** — both files say `GPL-3.0 (GNU General Public License Version 3)` (H77, violates H7 GPL-3.0 canonical; adds 2 more Apache strays); (b) 💭 **0/2** tier labels (H78, same recurring gap); (c) 💭 `FeedbackEntry` stores submitter PII (`submitter_name`/`submitter_contact` email+GitHub/`channel`) + `attachments` (paths/URLs) with no consent flag / anonymization / erasure hook, and `to_dict()` serializes PII wholesale (H79 — when the collector/channels implement transmission they must respect the §4.3 consent gate + erasure, and validate attachment paths for traversal). `__init__.py` also hacks `sys.path.insert(0, _this_dir)` + absolute `from models import` instead of relative imports (fold into H76).

- [ ] **[UmerOS] `fs/` study notes (v1.17).** `fs/` = **3 files (777 LOC)** — the **Virtual Filesystem (VFS) layer over a Quantum Filesystem (QFS)** content-addressable store: `qfs.py` (651 LOC — `CASStore` SHA3-256-addressed in-memory block store w/ dedup + refcount, `QFSCompressor` LZMA + XOR-delta `MAGIC=b"QFS1"`, `AIFileIndexer` keyword/TF-IDF search, `QFS` facade: write/read/delete/snapshot/restore/search/stats), `vfs.py` (124 LOC — `VirtualFileSystem` POSIX-path → QFS shim that user-space + Syscall-Shim use), `__init__.py` (2 LOC — clean relative re-exports). **This is the first folder in the whole study with a tier label present**: `qfs.py:2` docstring `[TODAY / EXPERIMENTAL]` (good — add the same to the other 2 files). **Security-clean**: no `subprocess`/`shell=True`/`eval`/`exec`/`pickle`/`ctypes`/`os.system`/`yaml.load` anywhere; `qfs.py` correctly uses `log = logging.getLogger("UmerOS.QFS")` + `threading.Lock` on every shared store (model discipline to copy). But off-baseline + zero-trust-gapped: (a) 🟡 **no capability gating on any fs mutation** — `vfs.py` `write_file`/`VirtualFileSystem.mkdir` and `qfs.py` `QFS.write_file`/`delete_file`/`snapshot`/`restore_snapshot` are callable by any in-process code with **no `CapabilityManager` check** (H80, same "privileged op / no gate" family as H27/H28/H46/H51/H60/H66/H73) — a user-space VFS write must be capability-gated before `[TODAY]`; (b) 🟡 **license split** — `qfs.py:27` says `GPL-3.0 (GNU General Public License Version 3)` (H81, H7 stray), while `vfs.py` + `__init__.py` have **no license header at all**, and `vfs.py` uses `print("[VFS]...")` instead of `logging` (per-file baseline gap, H81); (c) 💭 `qfs.py:snapshot()` reaches **into private state** `self._store._refs` (L580-583) — breaks `CASStore` encapsulation; should use a public refcount API (H82); (d) 💭 **CAS uses SHA3-256** (`hashlib.sha3_256`, qfs.py:84) but the design mandate specifies **SHA3-512** for UmerOS hashing (H82) — confirm whether 256-bit is an intentional CAS-addressing choice vs the 512-bit crypto mandate and document it. `vfs.py:_resolve` (L44-55) splits `path.strip("/").split("/")` and treats `..` as a literal child name (no up-traversal resolution/validation) — benign in an in-memory tree, but note for the real mount path.

- [ ] **[UmerOS] `home/` study notes (v1.18).** `home/` = **10 files (1,553 LOC)** — the **`/home` user-home-directory subsystem** (FHS 3.0): `home_manager.py` (`HomeManager` — create/remove home, XDG dirs, skel copy, disk usage, perms), `dotfiles.py` (`DotfilesManager` — install/remove/custom dotfiles from templates), `user_profile.py` (`UserProfileManager` — per-user env/aliases/PATH, writes `.profile`/`.bashrc`, loads `/etc/passwd`), `home_dirs.py` (`HomeDirsManager` — XDG + hidden dirs, `user-dirs.dirs`), `home_local.py` (`HomeLocalManager` — `~/.local/{share,bin,lib,state}`, installs binaries `chmod 0o755`), `home_ssh.py` (`HomeSSHManager` — `~/.ssh` keys/authorized_keys/known_hosts/config, perms 0o700/0o600), `home_mail.py` (`HomeMailManager` — `~/Maildir` + `/var/spool/mail`), `home_quota.py` (`HomeQuotaManager` — quota tracking), `home_backup.py` (`HomeBackupManager` — tar.gz backup/restore to `/var/backups/homes`), `__init__.py` (clean re-exports). **Injection-safe at the Python level**: no `subprocess`/`shell=True`/`eval`/`exec`/`pickle`/`ctypes`/`os.system`/`yaml.load` anywhere (it is path-based, not shell-based — good). **Good models**: `home_ssh` uses `secrets.token_hex` (CSPRNG, like `drivers/crypto.py`) + strict perms (.ssh 0o700, priv key 0o600, config 0o600); `home_manager`/`home_dirs` build a correct FHS/XDG layout. But off-baseline + zero-trust-gapped: (a) 🔴 **`home_backup.restore_backup` (L66-79) calls `tarfile.extractall(str(self.home_path))` with NO `filter`** — classic tar-traversal / arbitrary-file-write (CVE-2007-4559): a malicious or swapped `/var/backups/homes/<user>_*.tar.gz` extracts `../`/absolute/symlink members outside `/home`, and it `shutil.rmtree`s the live home *before* extracting (data-loss if the tar is bad) (H83); (b) 🟡 **systemic path traversal via unvalidated `username`** — nearly every method builds `self.home_path / username` (and `/username/.ssh` etc.) and `mkdir`/`write_text`/`rmtree`s with no segment validation, so `username="../etc"` escapes `/home`; 🔴 for destructive callers `remove_home`/`restore_backup`/`remove_user_dirs`/`remove_local` (H84, same family as H79 attachment paths); also `home_mail.deliver` uses `message.id` as a filename and `home_local.install_binary`/`get_local_path` use an unsanitized `name`/`subdir` segment; (c) 🟡 **no capability gating on privileged `/home` mutations** — create/remove home, SSH key + `authorized_keys` append, mail-spool write (`/var/spool/mail`), backup/restore all run with no `CapabilityManager` check (H85, same "privileged op / no gate" family as H27/H28/H46/H51/H60/H66/H73/H80); (d) 🟡 **`user_profile` interpolates unsanitized env-var values & aliases into sourced `.profile`/`.bashrc`** (`export KEY="VALUE"`, `alias n='cmd'`) — a value containing `"`/`\n`/backtick breaks out of the quote → shell injection / config poisoning when the profile is sourced (H86); (e) 🟡 **`home_ssh.generate_key` produces FAKE non-functional SSH keys** (`secrets.token_hex` dressed up as a PEM/ssh string — won't work with real OpenSSH; overstates security like H33/H49/H14) and `add_authorized_key` appends an **unsanitized** public key to `authorized_keys` (an unprivileged caller could append an attacker key → SSH access as that user); uses SHA-256 not the mandated SHA3-512 (H87); (f) 💭 **0/10 carry a tier label, 0/10 a license header, 0/10 `from __future__`, 0/10 `logging`** — the whole folder is silent (`_set_permissions`/`home_ssh` chmod errors are swallowed with `pass`); `user_profile` has an unused `import os`; disk-usage scans use `rglob('*')` (symlink-loop/perf risk) (H88).

- [ ] **[UmerOS] `HostFiles/` study notes (v1.19).** `HostFiles/` = **3 plain-text asset files (~347 bytes total)**: `welcome.txt` + `welcome_copy.txt` (both the single line `Welcome to Umer OS v2.0.0-Quantum`) and `chat_log.txt` (a one-paragraph philosophy blurb about zero-trust/AI/quantum — **no PII, no secrets, no code**). **This is a data/assets folder, NOT Python source**, so the per-file baseline (type hints/docstrings/logging/tier labels/license headers) and dangerous-call checks from §4 do **not apply** — recorded here for completeness per the standing "study every folder" convention. Findings: (a) 💭 **`welcome_copy.txt` is a byte-for-byte duplicate of `welcome.txt`** — a redundant dead asset (consolidate to one file, or delete the copy); (b) 💭 **`chat_log.txt` is misnamed** — it is not a chat log, just a marketing/philosophy paragraph (rename or relabel to avoid confusion); (c) 💭 the banner version string `v2.0.0-Quantum` should be confirmed against the canonical project version (e.g. `setup.py`/`pyproject`) so the welcome screen doesn't drift. Context: the only code reference is `ui/umer_de.py:16` (`HostBridge.HOST_DIR = os.getcwd()/"HostFiles"`), the **retired Tkinter DE (H25)** — it writes VFS files into this dir via `extract_and_open`; basename extraction (`vfs_path.strip('/').split('/')[-1]`) avoids traversal there. Once Flutter is canonical (H11/H25), this folder's only consumer goes away — treat `HostFiles/` as legacy-asset drift to prune with the Tkinter DE.

- [ ] **[UmerOS] `initrd/` study notes (v1.20).** `initrd/` = **17 Python modules (5,112 LOC)** — the **early-boot initial-RAM-disk runtime, builder & tools** that model the Linux `/initrd` eight phases: `linuxrc.py` (PID-1 orchestrator), `linuxrc_main.py`/`__main__.py`/`__init__.py` (entry points), `phase_machine.py` (8-phase state machine), `hooks.py` (initramfs-tools-style hook system), `ramdisk.py` (in-memory tmpfs stand-in + lifecycle), `vfs_ops.py` (in-memory VFS), `mounts.py` (mount/chroot/dev /proc /sys), `pivot_root.py` (new/old root swap), `cpio.py` (newc reader/writer), `archivers.py` (gzip/xz/lz4/zstd), `module_resolver.py` (module DB + autoprobe/hybrid), `ai_helper.py` (AI module hints + QRNG entropy), `scenarios.py` (install/live/recovery/rescue), `signals.py` (PID-1 signal layer), `builder.py` (cpio image builder). **Best baseline of any folder studied so far**: all 17 carry `from __future__ import annotations`, `logging.getLogger`, Google docstrings, and a `_selftest()`; all 17 use `Licence: GPL-3.0 (GNU General Public License Version 3)` (non-canonical, H7). It is the **most security-critical subsystem** (runs as PID 1 / uid 0 and performs pivot_root / mount / chroot / `seteuid(0)` / `exec /sbin/init`). Findings: (a) 🔴 **`ai_helper.py:166` `eval(line)`** over `/var/log/umeros_initrd_history.log` lines ("`# noqa: S307 - history file is trusted`") re-locates the known **H2** into the early-boot path — on a real host that log is attacker-writable → arbitrary code exec as uid 0 at boot; the "trusted" claim is a fail-open rationalization (same family as H17/H27/H28/H46/H51/H64) (H91); (b) 🔴 **no capability gating on privileged initrd ops** — `pivot_root`, `mount`, `chroot_into`, `linuxrc._drop_to_root` (`os.seteuid(0)`), `exec /sbin/init` run with **no `CapabilityManager` check**; the most sensitive location of the H27/H28/H46/H51/H60/H66/H73/H80/H85 gap (H92); (c) 🔴 **CPIO entry-name path traversal in `InitrdBuilder._unpack_to_dir` (`builder.py:342-360`)** — `entry.name` is taken verbatim from the cpio header with no `..` sanitization and `dest = target / rel` can escape `target` (arbitrary file write, CWE-22; mirror of tar traversal H83 for cpio — the in-memory path via `ramdisk._populate_from_entries` is SAFE because `vfs_ops._split` pops `..`) (H93); (d) 🟡 **dynamic `__import__` loads** — `ai_helper.py:155` `_try_import` (`__import__(name)`) and `__main__.py:66` (`__import__(mod, fromlist=["_selftest"])`) import by name with no validation; both are constrained to a fixed module list, so low risk, but should be allow-listed (H94); (e) 🟡 **Apache-2.0 stray across all 17 files** (violates H7 — a single sweep relicenses to GPL-3.0 since it is consistent) (H95); (f) 💭 **0/17 tier labels** — every module's maturity is undeclared (§4.4 mandatory rule) (H96); (g) 🟡 **crypto strength** — uses SHA-256 (`builder` image hashes L290/305, `ai_helper` entropy L135, `module_resolver` specs) where the design mandate is SHA3-512; CSPRNG (`os.urandom`) is good and entropy degrades gracefully, but the hash primitive is below the mandated standard for image-integrity/entropy (H97). **Positives to copy**: `vfs_ops._split` canonicalizes `..` (traversal-safe in-memory VFS — a model the production `fs/vfs.py` should copy, H80-82); `module_resolver.from_user_config` **fails CLOSED** on unknown module names (good contrast to the `ai_helper` eval fail-open); `signals.PID1SignalHandler.install` is opt-in via env var `UMEROS_INITRD_SIGNALS` (avoids Windows/non-PID-1 issues); the `hooks` system is robust (tag-ordered, fail-soft, `HookAbort` propagates); `phase_machine` enforces only legal phase transitions. 💭 **Design caveat**: `mounts.chroot_into` is explicitly documented as *not* security isolation (L31-33); combined with the uid-0 `INSTALL` scenario running a user-supplied installer (`install::run_installer`), a chroot escape = full root — keep installers capability-gated + sandboxed (overlaps H92).

- [ ] **[UmerOS] `installer/` study notes (v1.21).** `installer/` = **3 files (~22 KB total)** — the **deployment/install surface** for Umer OS: `installer.py` (17.9 KB — `UmerInstaller` full interactive wizard + `InstallLogger`), `install.py` (3.9 KB — a *second* `UmerInstaller` class, a non-functional stub), `__init__.py` (re-exports `install.py`). **Injection-safe at the Python level**: no `subprocess`/`shell=True`/`eval`/`exec`/`pickle`/`ctypes`/`os.system`/`yaml.load` anywhere (good). `installer.py` is a **good baseline model** — `from __future__`, `logging.getLogger`, Google docstrings, `InstallLogger` with UTC timestamps + OSError-guarded `flush`, EULA gate, `check_prerequisites` Python-version check, skips hidden dirs in `os.walk`, `show_eula` **fails closed** in non-interactive mode (`answer=""` → `False`), and `run()` catches OSError and calls `rollback()` on copy/bootloader failure. But it has real gaps: (a) 🔴 **two divergent `UmerInstaller` classes** — `__init__.py` re-exports `install.py`'s *stub* (its `install()` literally prints "Real installation would happen here" and does no copy/bootloader), so `from installer import UmerInstaller` gets the non-functional class while `installer.py` holds the real one (same duplication anti-pattern as H38/H106) (H98); (b) 🔴 **`install.py:display_waiver` fails OPEN** — auto-accepts the "I AGREE" liability waiver in `dry_run` (the *default*) AND silently `return True` when stdin is **non-TTY** (`else: return True`), bypassing the waiver non-interactively (same fail-open family as H29/H17/H27/H28/H46/H51/H64; also breaks `installer.py`'s own "non-negotiable" EULA rule #2) (H99); (c) 🔴 **`installer.py:rollback` `shutil.rmtree(self._install_root)` with NO path-guard / scope validation** and is auto-invoked on copy/bootloader failure — data-loss risk if `UMER_INSTALL_ROOT` is misconfigured or pointed at a non-UmerOS dir; no check it isn't `/` or a system dir (🔴 data-loss blocker) (H101); (d) 🟡 **no `CapabilityManager` gate** on privileged install ops (writes to `/opt/umer_os`, `/opt/umer_backup`, `/var/log`, FUTURE bootloader/GRUB/UEFI) — same family as H27/H28/H46/H51/H60/H66/H72/H73/H80/H85/H92 (H102); (e) 🟡 `copy_os_files` builds `dst_path = dst/rel_path` with **no `_safe_join`/`..` canonicalization** (no `vfs_ops._split`-style guard) and copies hidden files (only hidden dirs skipped) — traversal/clobber hardening (H103); (f) 🟡 licensing: `installer.py:25` says `GPL-3.0 (GNU General Public License Version 3)` (H7 stray), `install.py`/`__init__.py` have **no header** (H104); (g) 🟡 **no integrity/signature verification** of the OS files being installed (zero-trust gap; tie to H46/H97) (H108); (h) 🟡 **over-promised legal contract** — the EULA/waiver claims a pre-install *backup* + a *rollback that fully restores* state, but TODAY `backup_bootloader` only writes a JSON metadata snapshot (not MBR/GPT/EFI bytes) and `rollback` only `rmtree`s the install root (never restores boot/partition) (H109); (i) 💭 `install.py` skips the baseline (`print` not `logging`, no `from __future__`, no tier label) (H105); (j) 💭 `installer.py` lazy-imports stdlib (`datetime`/`multiprocessing`) inside methods — move top-level (H107). **Recommended fix order**: delete/merge `install.py` (H98/H106) → fail-CLOSED waiver in both (H99/H100) → guard `rollback` path (H101) → add `_safe_join` (H103) → capability gate (H102) → license/integrity sweep (H104/H108).

- [ ] **[UmerOS] `kernel/` study notes (v1.22).** `kernel/` = **40 files (~10,131 LOC)** — the **microkernel core** (`umer_kernel.py` 87 KB centerpiece + `shell_commands.py` 82 KB command registry) plus ~38 supporting modules. **Two quality tiers coexist:** (A) a **strong baseline cluster** — `capability_manager.py`, `ipc_bus.py`, `scheduler.py`, `panic.py`, `pid_allocator.py`, `signals.py`, `taint.py`, `sysctl.py`, `memory_manager.py`, `resource.py`, `softirq.py`, `reboot.py`, `audit.py`, `cred.py`, `workqueue.py`, `modules/dev/*` (udev) — all `from __future__` + `logging` + docstrings + `asyncio.Lock`/`threading.Lock`; `capability_manager.py` is the canonical zero-trust primitive (`check()` raises `PermissionError`, `SYSTEM_PID=0` omnipotent, thread-safe), `ipc_bus.py` is HMAC-SHA256-signed (`hmac.compare_digest`). (B) an **off-baseline toy/REPL cluster** — `container_engine.py`, `installer.py`, `umer_ai.py`, `qfs.py`, `quantum_sim.py`, `gui.py`, `cpuidle.py`, `power_manager.py`, `pqcrypto_.py` (runs Kyber keygen **at import**), `onnxruntime_.py`, `setup_umer_os.py` (calls async `kernel.boot()` without `await`), plus `bootloader.py` (duplicated triple header + `input()` EULA) — these use `print`/`input()`, no `from __future__`/logging/license/tier. **Headline finding 🔴 H110:** the live `UmerKernel.__init__` (L674-676) wires **no-op placeholder stubs** for `MemoryManager`, `IPCBus`, and `CapabilityManager` (`type('X', (), {...})()`) — the correctly-built real modules exist but are **not imported** (the correct wiring `MemoryManager()`/`IPCBus()`/`CapabilityManager()` is commented out at L1569-1571). So zero-trust capability gating, HMAC-signed IPC, and real memory accounting are all **inert** — the most important occurrence yet of the fail-open / weak-default-trust family (H17/H27/H28/H46/H51/H60/H66/H73/H80/H85/H92/H98/H99/H101). 🔴 H111 `CryptoEngine` (L429-434) is a dummy: `verify` returns `True` unconditionally, `sign` returns `b"dummy_signature"` (crypto fail-open). 🔴 H112 `SecuritySandbox.register_process` only `print`s (no sandboxing). 🟡 H113 `sudo`/`su` (shell_commands.py:1211/1229) set `current_user="root"` with no auth/capability check; 🟡 H114 `kill`/`shutdown`/`mount`/`modprobe`/`chmod`/`chown` run un-gated; 🟡 H115 capability lifecycle (grant/check/revoke) entirely unwired (`scheduler.exit_task` docstring claims "revoke capabilities" but holds no `CapabilityManager`); 🟡 H116 GUI launched via `subprocess.Popen` of legacy `ui/launch_gui.py` (not Flutter); 🟡 H117 `ipc_bus.try_receive` skips HMAC verify + no capability gate (latent once H110 fixed); 🟡 H118 `cred.is_root()` (euid==0) bypasses capability checks (collapses to root-trust with H113); 🟡 H119 `cgroup.check_memory_limit` returns `True` (allow) for ungrouped PIDs. 💭 H120 dependency on `umer_kernel1.py`; 💭 H121 `bootloader.py` triple header + `input()`; 💭 H122 `gui.py` Kivy contradicts Flutter (H11/H25); 💭 H123 off-baseline toy modules listed above; 💭 H124 `pqcrypto_.py`/`onnxruntime_.py` execute at import; 💭 H125 `requirements.txt` lists `kivy` (not Flutter) + `cryptography` unused by dummy `CryptoEngine`; 💭 H126 `udev_rules` advertises `RUN` (unimplemented) + device nodes default `0o666`; 🟡 H127 `panic()` calls `self.taint.add("TAINT_KERNEL_PANIC")` — a string **not in** `KernelTaint._FLAG_INDEX`, so `add()` raises `ValueError` and the panic path crashes on itself (latent bug). **Models to copy:** `capability_manager.py` (real `check()`), `ipc_bus.py` HMAC verify, `scheduler.py` watchdog/lock discipline.

- [ ] **[UmerOS] `legal/` study notes (v1.23).** `legal/` = **12 files (92 KB)** — a **legal/compliance/consent Python package** (not just docs): `manager.py` (`LegalManager` + `get_default_legal_manager`), `cli.py` (`legal_ctl` argparse CLI), `disclaimer.py` (`DisclaimerRegistry`/`RiskLevel` — clean, well-structured), `consent.py` (`ConsentManager` + cryptographic consent ledger), `contributors.py` (`ContributorRegistry` + DCO), `donations.py` (`DonationsManager` — hardcoded creator IBANs/phones/emails + sponsor tiers), `licenses.py` (`LicenseManager` — multi-license scanner), `maintainers.py` (`MaintainerRegistry` — PQC/PGP fingerprints), `safety_check.py` (`SafetyChecker` — pre-exec backup), `test_legal.py` (8-test suite), `README.md`, `__init__.py` (package init w/ `sys.path` hack). **Baseline is otherwise good** — 10/12 `.py` have `from __future__` + `logging` + type hints + docstrings; **no `subprocess`/`eval`/`exec`/`shell=True`/`pickle` anywhere** (injection-safe). But it is the **single largest Apache-2.0 cluster** (all 11 `.py` headers + `licenses.py` docstring say "GPL-3.0 (GNU General Public License Version 3)", and `README.md:40` defines the project license set as Apache-2.0/GPL-2.0/MIT with **no GPL-3.0** — directly contradicting the adopted **H7 → GPL-3.0 canonical** decision; this folder is where H7 must be resolved). **Systemic fail-open / overstated-claim pattern** (same family as H17/H27/H28/H29/H46/H51/H99/H111): 🔴 **H128** `licenses.py` codifies Apache-2.0 as *primary* and has **no GPL-3.0 text entry** + never detects GPL-3.0 (only `"GPL"`→`GPL-2.0`); 🔴 **H129** `scan_directory` is a **fail-open compliance audit** — any file containing "Licence"/"License"/"Copyright" is counted *compliant* (→ "Custom/Proprietary"), so proprietary/unknown licenses pass and the GPL-3.0 mandate can't be enforced; 🔴 **H130** `get_license_text(name)` **silently returns Apache-2.0 text for any unknown name incl. "GPL-3.0"** (no real canonical text); 🔴 **H131** `consent.require_consent_interactive` **fails OPEN** — auto-grants in `dry_run` and in any non-TTY env (calls `grant_consent("I AGREE")` with no user input), so the mandatory "I AGREE" gate is bypassed in automation (contrast `installer.py` which fails closed); 🔴 **H135** `cli.py` `consent` subcommand **hardcodes `user_response="I AGREE"`** → `python -m legal.cli consent` auto-grants with no user action, and `test_legal.py:229` encodes this as expected; 🟡 **H132** the "cryptographic" consent token is `sha256` of *public* values with no key — forgeable, and the ledger isn't integrity-verified on load (overstated-claim, cf. H51/H111); 🟡 **H133** `consent.DEFAULT_LEDGER_PATH` is a hardcoded dev Windows path; 🟡 **H134** consent recorded under default "admin" with no identity check (PII in JSON ledger, §4.3); 🟡 **H136** `contributors.verify_dco` is decorative (`dco_signed` defaults `True`, never really verified); 🟡 **H138** `maintainers` PQC/PGP fingerprints are **hardcoded static literals** (forgeable "cryptographic identity"); 🟡 **H139** `safety_check.verify_safety` defaults `is_safe=True` and only blocks on <500 MB free or a *CRITICAL*-level backup failure (HIGH/MODERATE ops proceed with a failed checkpoint backup — soft/fail-open gate); 🟡/🔴 **H140** `test_legal.py` Test 4 asserts `verify_dco("Antigravity AI / DeepMind Team")` is `True` but that name **isn't in the roster** → the test *fails as written* (no CI to catch it, cf. H16); 🟡 **H141** tests encode the fail-open `consent` CLI and don't cover `require_consent_interactive`'s non-TTY auto-grant, license-audit accuracy, or safety-blocking; 💭 **H137** real personal PII/financial data hardcoded (`mumeryasin123456789@gmail.com` in `contributors.py`/`maintainers.py`; full IBANs/phones/emails/Wise/Payoneer in `donations.py`); 💭 **H142** `DonationTier` labels mismatch code thresholds (SILVER "$50+" vs `>=500`); 💭 **H143** `ContributorRole(str)` should be an `Enum`; disclaimer text L86 "DEEPMIND AI ASSISTANTS" copy-paste artifact; 💭 **H144** `licenses.py` reads 2 KB + `errors="ignore"`, `sys.path` import hack, **0/12 `legal/` files carry a tier label**, `README.md:131` references `tests/test_legal.py` (not wired into `tests/`); 💭 **H145** relative imports rely on `sys.path.insert` instead of package-relative `from .consent import ...`. **Fix order:** resolve H7 here (make GPL-3.0 canonical in `licenses.py`+headers+README, add GPL-3.0 text + detection) → flip consent gate fail-closed (H131/H135) → make DCO/fingerprints real or stop claiming them (H136/H138) → fail-closed license audit + correct `get_license_text` (H129/H130) → fix the broken test (H140/H141) → harden safety gate (H139).

- [ ] **[UmerOS] `lib/` study notes (v1.24).** `lib/` = **33 source modules (25 top-level `.py` + 8 in `lostfound/`)** — a **faithful FHS `/lib` simulation**: shared-library / ELF / dynamic-linker / kernel-module / filesystem-layout modeling. Modules: `security.py` (PAM stub registry), `ssl_libs.py` (SSL/TLS store mgmt), `kernel_modules.py` (depmod/modprobe/`/lib/modules`), `dynamic_linker.py` (`ld.so.conf` parser + binary `ld.so.cache` writer/reader), `elf_parser.py` (real ELF parser), `fhs.py` ( audit + bootstrap), `usr_lib.py`/`usr_include.py` (usr trees), `var_lib.py` (`/var/lib` + alternatives), `arch.py`/`multiarch.py`/`essential_libs.py`/`library_manager.py`/`ldd.py`/`iconv.py`/`kbd.py`/`iptables_libs.py`/`oss.py`/`firmware.py`/`tmpfiles.py`/`libinfo.py`/`__main__.py`; `lostfound/` = `fsck`/`inode`/`superblock`/`partition`/`orphan` (ext4 e2fsck simulation). **Best-disciplined folder in the whole study**: every module has `from __future__ import annotations` + `logging.getLogger` + type hints + Google docstrings + a `_selftest()`; **zero dangerous calls anywhere** — no `subprocess`/`os.system`/`eval`/`exec`/`pickle`/`ctypes`/`shell=True`/`marshal`/`shelve`/`yaml.load` (grep-verified across all 33). `dynamic_linker.py` parses the glibc `ld.so.cache` binary format with correct bounds (string-table offset) — safe. `lostfound/fsck.py` is a model **fail-closed** checker: marks the filesystem clean only when `superblock_ok and unresolved_orphans == 0 and errors_found == errors_fixed`; `fhs.py` audit is conservative (`ok` only when no ERROR). **Findings:** (a) 🔴 **H3 re-confirmed** — `security.py:72` `PASSWORD = "password"` is a hardcoded module-level constant (latent: not referenced in any active auth path, but a hardcoded-weak-credential smell; remove/replace); (b) 🔴 **H146** `ssl_libs.py:414-427` `_check_is_trusted` returns `True` if ANY CA bundle file exists on disk ("If store exists, assume basic trust") + `check_trust` (L225-245) trusts any `is_ca` cert and only string-matches `issuer==subject` — **trust/verify fail-open** (same family as H17/H111/H128/H129); (c) 🔴 **H147** `ssl_libs.py:82-92` `is_expired` always `False` + `days_until_expiry` hardcoded `365` — **certificates never expire** (expiry fail-open, H111 family); (d) 💭 **H148** `ssl_libs.py` is a "simplified" string-parsed sim — no `cryptography`/OpenSSL, loose `is_ca` heuristic, ships as if production-ready (needs `[EXPERIMENTAL]` tier); (e) 🟡 **H149** ~23 `lib/` modules + `README.md:138` carry `GPL-3.0 (GNU General Public License Version 3)` / "GPL-3.0 (GNU General Public License Version 3) - same as the rest of Umer OS" — **largest Apache-2.0 cluster, contradicts H7 GPL-3.0 canonical**; (f) 💭 **H150** `security.py` registers `pam_permit.so` "Always permit" as a stock PAM module — low risk (metadata) but a fail-open trap if the `security/` subsystem ever consults this registry for auth. **Fix order:** fail-closed `_check_is_trusted` + real expiry → resolve H7 (relicense to GPL-3.0) → remove H3 + add H150 caveat.

- [ ] **[UmerOS] `liboqs/` study notes (v1.25).** `liboqs/` = **upstream Open Quantum Safe (OQS) C library, vendored UNPINNED into UmerOS** (no `.gitmodules` at repo root -> can silently drift from upstream; MIT license, conflicts with H7 GPL-3.0 canonical). Scope: **5,665 `.c`/`.h` files** under `src/` - real, externally-audited PQC (constant-time; FIPS-203/204/205 aligned). KEM families: `ml_kem` (ML-KEM/Kyber, FIPS-203), `kyber`, `bike`, `classic_mceliece`, `frodokem`, `hqc`, `ntru`, `ntruprime`; SIG: `ml_dsa` (ML-DSA/Dilithium, FIPS-204), `slh_dsa` (SPHINCS+, FIPS-205), `falcon`, `cross`, `mayo`, `mqom`, `snova`, `uov`. **Supplies the §4.2 zero-trust PQC mandate** (SHA3-512, Kyber/Dilithium, AES-256-GCM) only if UmerOS binds to it; the C folder is **not imported by the Python runtime** (see H155). **Python surface (~28 `.py`, reviewable):** upstream `scripts/` (docs/CBOM/upstream-copy) + `tests/` (KAT/Wycheproof/ACVP/valgrind harnesses) - third-party, not UmerOS-authored, so the Python baseline doesn't apply, but they sit in the vendored tree. **Dangerous-call scan:** `subprocess.run` in `scripts/copy_from_upstream/*.py` + `tests/helpers.py` - all list-args, zero `shell=True`; no `eval`/`exec`/`pickle`/`ctypes`/`yaml.load`/`__import__`. `return True` hits are benign (CBOM "needs-update" flag; feature-detection helpers). **License conflict (H7):** `liboqs/LICENSE.txt` = MIT, conflicting with adopted GPL-3.0. **But the live PQC path doesn't use it:** `quantum/crypto_pqc.py` (`import oqs`->`liboqs-python`; `[TODAY]`-tier, **Apache-2.0 stray H7**, **silent classical-crypto fallback** when liboqs-python missing -> H152 🔴) + `kernel/pqcrypto_.py` (commented `pqcrypto` *example*, divergent pure-Python backend -> H153 🟡); 🔴 H154 `cloud/ota_updater/update_system.py:33` hardcodes `simulated_dilithium_sig_abc123` in a "verify signature" step. 💭 **H155** vendored `liboqs/` is dead/unwired (integration gap). **Fix order:** pin the vendored lib (`.gitmodules`) -> relicense to GPL-3.0 -> bind live PQC to the C lib -> remove the silent classical fallback -> wire OTA to the real `CryptoEngine.verify`.
- [ ] **[UmerOS] `media/` study notes (v1.26).** `media/` = **12 FHS `/media` removable-media subsystem modules** (`media_types`, `mount_manager`, `device_info`, `cleanup`, `hotplug`, `filesystem`, `mount_ops`, `auto_mount`, `permissions`, `fstab`, `udisks2`, `__init__`). Generally well-disciplined: full baseline (from __future__ + logging + type hints + Google docstrings), `subprocess` arg-list everywhere (no `shell=True`; device_info blkid, mount_ops real mount), no `eval`/`exec`/`pickle`/`ctypes`/`yaml.load` except `cleanup.py:72` `__import__("re")` anti-pattern; cleanup safely scoped (regex-validated top-level `/media` only, unlinks symlinks without following, no `rmtree`). **But the privileged mount/auto-mount path is zero-trust-gapped and bypasses the hardening that exists:** 🔴 H156 no `CapabilityManager` gate on `mount_ops.mount`/`auto_mount._handle_hotplug`/`udisks2.UDisks2Client.mount`; 🔴 H157 `auto_mount._do_mount` mounts removable media `rw` without `noexec,nodev,nosuid` (bypasses `MountManager.allocate` which sets them + `filesystem.mount_options_for(removable=True)`, setuid-on-USB -> priv-esc); 🟡 H158 `permissions.py` authz unwired (nothing calls it before mount) + `" optical"` literal bug + `require_primary_group` never consulted; 🟡 H159 `fstab.FstabManager.add()` skips `validate()` (detect-but-don't-fail-closed) + `make_removable_entry` omits hardening + `save()` un-gated; 🟡 H160 5 Apache-2.0 strays + 7 missing headers (H7); 💭 H161 3 broken (`device_info`/`cleanup`/`hotplug` crash on `__main__`) + 1 non-conformant (`mount_manager`) `_selftest()`; 💭 H162 `cleanup.py __import__("re")` (scoping safe); 💭 H163 `filesystem.FsType.NVME` nonexistent enum + `EXT4` dup; 💭 H164 `mount_ops._real_unmount` dead code + lazy flag bug; 💭 H165 `auto_mount.user_mode` decorative + auto-mount with no consent. **Models to copy:** `MountManager.allocate()` (L243) + `mount_options_for(removable=True)` (L407) already add `noexec,nosuid,nodev`; `fstab.FstabValidator` exists - enforce it. **Fix order:** capability-gate mount/auto-mount/udisks2 -> wire `auto_mount`/`udisks2` through `MountManager.allocate()` + `mount_options_for(removable=True)` -> wire `permissions.py` -> enforce `fstab.validate()` -> fix 4 `_selftest()`s + `FsType.NVME` + `" optical"` + dead `_real_unmount` -> resolve H7.
- [ ] **[UmerOS] `mnt/` study notes (v1.27).** `mnt/` = **7 FHS `/mnt` admin-mount subsystem modules** (`mount_ops`, `fstab`, `mount_point`, `user_mount`, `audit`, `validation`, `__init__`). The privileged counterpart to `/media` (admin/static mounts vs auto-detected removable). **More disciplined than media/**: every module has the full baseline (from __future__ + logging + type hints + Google docstrings); **all 7 `_selftest()`s are FUNCTIONAL** (no broken `_selftest()`s like media/hotplug); `subprocess` arg-list everywhere (no `shell=True`/`eval`/`exec`/`pickle`/`ctypes`/`yaml.load`); `audit.py` is a model - JSONL deserialization with key-filtering (`{k: v for k, v in data.items() if k in cls.__dataclass_fields__}`, no `eval`), append-only, safe `rotate()`. `validation.py` + `mount_point.py` validate names with strict regex `^[a-zA-Z0-9._-]+$` and flag symlinks. **But it repeats the media/ zero-trust gaps on the privileged mount path and adds one new destructive-op risk:** 🔴 H166 no `CapabilityManager` gate on `MountManager.mount`/`umount`/`remount`/`MountPointManager.create`/`remove`/`Fstab.write_file` (writes `/etc/fstab`); 🔴 H167 `MountPointManager.remove(force=True)` calls `shutil.rmtree(path)` on a path only `os.path.normpath`-validated (not `realpath`/symlink-checked) and `_validate_path` is NOT called in `remove` -> TOCTOU symlink swap -> arbitrary directory deletion (the one genuinely new hotspot in `mnt/`); 🔴 H168 `Fstab.write_file` un-gated privileged `/etc/fstab` write + `to_string()` drops `_comments`/`_header` (round-trip data loss). 🟡 H169 user-mountable `/mnt` entries (`fstab.make_user_mount`/`user_mount.make_fstab_entry`/`user_mount.user_mount`) add `nosuid`+`nodev` but NOT `noexec`, and `MountManager` never auto-applies `noexec,nosuid,nodev` for `/mnt` despite `is_under_mnt` (sibling of media H157); 🟡 H170 `MntValidator` findings advisory-only - `MountManager.mount()` never consults it (detect-but-don't-fail-closed, H159 family); 🟡 H171 `UserMountManager._save_mtab` rewrites the entire `/etc/mtab` from a partial in-memory list (`open(path,"w")` full overwrite) -> clobbers other entries / races; 💭 H172 `_enforce_user` stored but never consulted; 💭 H173 `fstab.py:int()` on `dump`/`pass_num` with no `try/except` -> uncaught `ValueError` aborts parse; 💭 H174 `Fstab.to_string()` serializes only entries, drops `_comments`/`_header`; 💭 H175 `mount_ops.mount`/`umount` build shell-shaped command *strings* (only logged, not run) - misleading logging pattern; 🟡 H176 6 Apache-2.0 strays (`Licence: GPL-3.0 (GNU General Public License Version 3)`, British spelling) + missing `__init__` header (H7). **Models to copy:** `audit.py` key-filtered JSONL deserialize (no `eval`); `validation.py`/`mount_point.py` strict name regex + symlink flag. **Fix order:** capability-gate mount/remove/fstab-write -> symlink/realpath guard on `remove(force=True)` -> auto-apply `noexec,nosuid,nodev` for `/mnt` -> enforce `MntValidator` -> fix mtab clobber -> resolve H7.
- [ ] **[UmerOS] `network/` study notes (v1.28).** `network/` = **6 FHS-style networking subsystem modules (~37 KB)**: `dns_resolver.py` (`DNSResolver` async resolver + TTL cache), `http_client.py` (`HTTPClient` aiohttp/urllib facade + `HTTPResponse`), `network_stack.py` (`NetworkStack` top-level manager + `DNSOverHTTPS` DoH, `VPNClient` WireGuard wrapper, `MDNSDiscovery`, `AINetworkQoS`, `InternetAccessManager`), `tcp_server.py` (`TCPServer`/`TCPClient` asyncio sockets), `vpn_tunnel.py` (`VPNTunnel` simulated tunnel), `__init__.py` (re-exports). **Generally well-disciplined baseline** (shebang + `from __future__` + `logging` + type hints + Google docstrings + tier labels on all 5 modules; injection-safe: `subprocess.run` **argv-list only, zero `shell=True`** in `VPNClient`; **no `eval`/`exec`/`os.system`/`pickle`/`ctypes`/`yaml.load`**); TLS is **verified by default** on both `urllib` (`urlopen` w/ system trust) and `aiohttp` paths (no `ssl.CERT_NONE`), and `http_client` caps body size (`max_response_bytes`) for DoS protection. **But the entire egress surface is zero-trust-gapped and one "encryption" is a footgun**: (a) 🔴 **no `CapabilityManager` gate on ANY network egress** - `DNSResolver.resolve*`/`resolve_all`/`reverse_lookup`, `HTTPClient.request`/`get`/`post_json`/`fetch_json`/`download`, `NetworkStack.connect`/`send_tcp`/`fetch_url`/`resolve`, `InternetAccessManager.*`, `VPNClient.connect`/`disconnect`, `TCPClient.connect` all perform privileged network ops with no capability check (same "privileged op / no gate" family as H27/H28/H46/H51/H60/H66/H73/H80/H85/H92/H110/H156/H166, **H177**); (b) 🔴 **`HTTPClient._validate_url` (http_client.py:227) does not block loopback/link-local/private ranges -> SSRF** - only scheme in {http,https} + netloc presence are checked, so `http://127.0.0.1`, `http://[::1]`, `http://169.254.169.254` (cloud metadata) and `http://10.0.0.1` all pass; raw `NetworkStack.connect`/`send_tcp` have no host-range guard either - the egress client is the zero-trust boundary and omitting internal-range blocking enables cross-tenant / metadata access (**H178**); (c) 🟡 **`VPNTunnel._xor_frame` (vpn_tunnel.py:92) is reversible XOR, not encryption** - default `VPNTunnel()` (no crypto_engine) "encrypts" with a repeating-key XOR that is malleable and known-plaintext-recoverable; honestly labeled "simulation" (contrast kernel H111 dummy `CryptoEngine.verify->True` which was deceptive), but a footgun if used as real transport -> should warn/refuse real egress without a real crypto engine (**H179**); (d) 🟡 **DNS resolution is plaintext/system-resolver, not authenticated** - `DNSResolver` uses the host resolver (no DoH/DNSSEC), while `DNSOverHTTPS` exists but is NOT the default for `DNSResolver` and is never enforced as policy -> spoofable answers feed `NetworkStack.connect` (zero-trust/PQC gap, **H180**); (e) 💭 **no license headers + `__init__.py` lacks `from __future__ import annotations`** in all 6 files (contrast mnt/ which had Apache-2.0 strays H176; here there is simply NO header - H7 canonical missing, **H181**); (f) 💭 **no `_selftest()` in any `network/` module** (contrast mnt/ where all 7 were functional -> regression safety reduced, **H182**). **Models to copy**: `tcp_server.py` is a model for abuse controls (`MAX_CONNECTIONS=100`, `MAX_CONNECTIONS_PER_IP=10`, `READ_TIMEOUT=30`, `MAX_MESSAGE_SIZE=65536`, per-IP `_check_rate_limit`) - reference it from §4; `VPNClient` uses argv-list `subprocess` (injection-safe); `VPNTunnel` is transparently labeled "simulation" (good disclosure practice). **Fix order**: capability-gate all egress (route through `CapabilityManager`) -> add connect-time egress policy that resolves + rejects loopback/link-local/private (SSRF) -> replace `VPNTunnel` XOR fallback with a real AEAD or refuse-no-engine -> make DoH/DNSSEC the default authenticated resolver -> add GPL headers + `__init__` future import -> add `_selftest()`s (start with `tcp_server` + `dns_resolver`).
- [ ] **[UmerOS] `opt/` study notes (v1.29).** `opt/` = **14 FHS `/opt` add-on-software modules** (`__init__`, `config`, `env`, `fhs`, `hierarchy`, `integration`, `manager`, `package`, `var`, `installers/` ×4 demo scripts, `test_opt`) - the package manager for `/opt`, `/etc/opt`, `/var/opt`. **Better baseline than media/mnt/network**: 4 modules (`env`/`fhs`/`hierarchy`/`var`) have `from __future__` + `logging` + functional `_selftest()`; `fhs.OptFHSValidator` is a model (read-only auditor, world-writable scan), `hierarchy` registry uses key-filtered `from_dict`, `var`/`env` discovery is scoped. **But the privileged install/remove/write surface is zero-trust-gapped and has concrete path-traversal + command-injection bugs:** 🔴 **H184** no `CapabilityManager` gate on ANY privileged op (install/remove/update/bootstrap/write_profile_d/registry/rmtree) - installs code that becomes a `$PATH` entry (high blast radius); 🔴 **H185** `var.write_file`/`read_file` (`filename`) + `config.install_config`/`get_config` (`config_file`/`package_name`) unvalidated → arbitrary file read/write outside `/var/opt` & `/etc/opt`; 🔴 **H186** `shutil.rmtree` on unvalidated `name`/`provider` across `manager.remove`/`package.OptManager.remove_package`/`OptPackage.remove`/`config.remove_*`/`var.remove_package_dir` → rmtree of an arbitrary directory (e.g. `name="../../etc"`); 🔴 **H187** `OptPackage.create_launcher_script`/`create_wrapper_script` interpolate `command`/`args`/`environment` unescaped into a `#!/bin/bash` script → code execution when run (script lands in `/opt/<pkg>/bin` which `env` adds to `$PATH`). 🟡 **H188** `env.write_profile_d` + `integration.OptPathManager` interpolate discovered `bin_path` unescaped into `export PATH="..."` written to `/etc/profile.d` + `~/.bashrc` (login-sourced injection); 🟡 **H189** `OptPackage` hardcodes real `/etc/opt` & `/var/opt` (ignores `opt_root` override) → real side effects even in temp-root runs; 🟡 **H193** `OptServiceManager` `chmod 0o755` + `subprocess.run([script,"start"])` on any discovered script (executes dropped-in content). 💭 **H190** `verify_integrity` only checks dir existence but is surfaced as "integrity" (no hashing/signing - false assurance); 💭 **H191** missing `from __future__` in config/manager/package/integration/installers/test_opt; 💭 **H192** no `_selftest()` in those (and `test_opt.py` is a print-runner that ignores the per-module `_selftest()`s + has zero security coverage). **Models to copy**: `fhs.OptFHSValidator`, `hierarchy` registry key-filtering, `env` discovery + functional `_selftest()`. **Fix order**: capability-gate all privileged ops → validate `name`/`provider`/`filename`/`config_file` (reject `..`, use `realpath` under root) → shell-escape/`shlex.quote` launcher & profile snippets → make `OptPackage` honor `opt_root` → real integrity (hash/sign) → resolve H7 + `from __future__` + `_selftest()`s (wire `test_opt` to call them).

- [ ] **[UmerOS] `packages/` study notes (v1.30).** `packages/` = **3 modules (635 LOC)** — the **user-space package manager** (`umer_pkg.py` `UmerPackageManager`/`PackageManifest`/`DependencyResolver`, `repository.py` `PackageRepository`/`PackageInfo`, `__init__`). **Best baseline of the small folders**: `umer_pkg.py` has `from __future__` + `logging` + type hints + Google docstrings + a **correct topological `DependencyResolver` with cycle detection** and **atomic snapshot+rollback** on install failure; `_verify_hash` fails **CLOSED** on mismatch (refuses install); `hashlib.sha3_256` is real (not dummy). `repository.py` is a clean in-memory catalog (no privileged ops). **But the install/extract surface has concrete high-severity bugs**: 🔴 **H194** tar-slip — `tarfile.extractall` without `filter=` + naive `files/` prefix → `../`/absolute/symlink traversal (arbitrary write, CVE-2007-4559 family); 🔴 **H195** untrusted manifest `name`/`version` → attacker-controlled `dest`/filename path traversal; 🔴 **H196** "Signed" overstated — only SHA3 self-hash, NO signature + `_verify_hash` fails OPEN when HASH absent; 🔴 **H197** integrity check hashes only `manifest.json`, never the `files/` payload (tamper-undetectable); 🔴 **H198** no `CapabilityManager` gate on `install`/`remove`/`update` (docstring claims admin grant — absent; user-space scope). 🟡 **H199** lexicographic version compare in `update` → upgrades silently skipped; 🟡 **H200** H7 `Licence: GPL-3.0 (GNU General Public License Version 3)` in `umer_pkg.py` + 2 files no header; 🟡 **H201** `_find_in_registry` fuzzy `startswith(name)` match; 🟡 **H204** symlink/hardlink extraction + no permission `filter`. 💭 **H202** missing `from __future__` in 2 modules; 💭 **H203** no `_selftest()` + `print` in `repository.py`. **Fix order**: `tarfile.extractall(..., filter="data")` + `realpath` scope check → validate manifest `name`/`version` charset → verify against pinned public-key signature (fail CLOSED) + hash the `files/` tree → capability-gate install/remove/update → semantic version compare → exact registry match → GPL headers + future import + `_selftest()`s.
- [ ] **[UmerOS] `proc/` study notes (v1.31).** `proc/` = **24 FHS `/proc` virtual-filesystem modules (3,203 LOC)** (`__init__`, `procfs`, `nodes`, `kernel_adapter`, `pid_entries`, `sysctl_fs`, `system_files`, `modules`, `mounts`, `cpuinfo`, `meminfo`, `loadavg`, `uptime`, `version`, `info`, `interrupts`, `ioports`, `dma`, `filesystems`, `partitions`, `swaps`, `net_files`, `sysvipc`, `tty_files`, `utils`). **Cleanest folder in the tree for dangerous-call hygiene**: grep across all 24 files found **ZERO** `subprocess`/`eval`/`exec`/`shell=True`/`os.system`/`pickle`/`ctypes`/`yaml.load`/`__import__`/`tarfile`/`shutil.rmtree`/`chmod`/`CapabilityManager`; nearly everything is a read-only virtual file generated by a handler. **But the writable surface is entirely zero-trust-gapped** (same "privileged op / no gate" family as H156/H166/H177/H184/H194/H198): 🔴 **H205** `procfs.write` (`procfs.py:177`) + `ProcFile.write` (`nodes.py:95`) enforce only per-file read-only `mode` (`PermissionError` when `_write is None`) — **no UID/owner check and no `CapabilityManager`** anywhere on the write path; `mode="rw-r--r--"` is cosmetic; 🔴 **H206** `/proc/sys/*` sysctl mutation (`sysctl_fs.py`) — ~60 writable params (kernel.hostname/panic_timeout, vm.drop_caches/swappiness/overcommit_memory, net.ipv4.ip_forward/tcp_syncookies/icmp_echo_ignore_all, fs.file-max/pipe-max-size, net.core.somaxconn) rewritten via `setattr(adapter,…)`/`registry.set(…)` with no `CAP_SYS_ADMIN`/`CAP_NET_ADMIN` gate -> posture-weakening / DoS primitive (disable syncookies, enable forwarding, drop caches, set panic_timeout); 🔴 **H207** per-PID `oom_score_adj` writable (`pid_entries.py:258`) via `adapter.oom_adj.__setitem__` with no cap gate -> re-weight OOM killer for any pid (evade OOM-protect / deny-memory); 🔴 **H208** `/proc/irq/<n>/smp_affinity` writable (`system_files.py:524`) via `adapter.irq_affinity.__setitem__` with no cap gate (`CAP_SYS_NICE`/`CAP_SYS_ADMIN` on real Linux) -> degrade I/O determinism. 🟡 **H209** several `/proc/sys/kernel/*` entries declared `mode="rw-r--r--"` but `write=lambda text: None` (system_files.py:103/105/107/115/129/131; sysctl_fs.py:103/105/107/115/129/131) — silent no-ops, contract mismatch with docstring "writes are validated and persisted"; 🟡 **H210** `sysctl_fs.py:12` docstring claims sysctl persisted by `SysctlRegistry` but hostname/domainname bypass via `setattr(adapter,…)` (L89/93) AND persist a trailing newline (`text.strip() + "\n"`) so reads return `"host\n"`; 🟡 **H211** `pid_entries.py` `status` hardcodes `CapEff: 0000003fffffffff` for uid==0 and `environ` is fully hardcoded — fabricated "full caps" + fake env can mislead auditors/tooling reading `/proc/<pid>/status` (fine for sim, label [EXPERIMENTAL]). 💭 **H212** `proc/` is a strong baseline for a read-mostly virtual FS — only the missing write gate is a real risk;
