💭 **H213** GPL-3.0 license headers absent across proc/ modules (spot-checked procfs/nodes/sysctl_fs/pid_entries/kernel_adapter/system_files/modules/mounts show `from __future__` but NO header — adopt H7 canonical + add); 💭 **H214** `modules.py`/`mounts.py` fall back to hardcoded simulated data when host `/proc` is unreadable (graceful, expected since UmerOS runs atop a real Linux kernel in userspace). **Fix order**: gate `ProcFileSystem.write` + the `writable_func`/`setattr`/`__setitem__` sinks behind `CapabilityManager.require("sys_admin")` (net.* -> `cap_net_admin`, oom -> `cap_sys_admin`, irq -> `cap_sys_nice`) -> enforce per-file owner/UID -> make no-op writes fail closed (or drop the rw mode) -> strip trailing newline on hostname/domainname -> clearly label simulated `CapEff`/`environ` -> add GPL headers.

- [ ] **[UmerOS] `quantum/` study notes (v1.32).** `quantum/` = **62 modules (~24,641 LOC)** — a **quantum-computing SDK/simulator** (`crypto_pqc`, `qkd`, `qrng`, `cli`, `quantum_sim`, `backend`, `quantum_server`, `quantum_api`, `providers/` IBM/IonQ/Braket/Rigetti adapters, `cloud/` auth+session, `compiler/`, `QISKIT_RESEARCH/` 3 markdown reports). **Strong dangerous-call hygiene** — grep across all 62 files found **ZERO** `subprocess`/`eval`/`exec`/`shell=True`/`os.system`/`pickle`/`ctypes`/`yaml.load`/`__import__`/`tarfile`/`shutil.rmtree`/`chmod`/`CapabilityManager`; `providers/base.py` even redacts tokens in `__repr__` (`token={'***' if self.token else None}`). **But the security surface is crypto/secret/credential-centric and has 4 🔴 + 3 🟡 + 4 💭 findings**: 🔴 **H215** Apache-2.0 header stray `GPL-3.0 (GNU General Public License Version 3)` (`crypto_pqc.py:22`, H7 family); 🔴 **H216** `PostQuantumCrypto` silently downgrades to `_FallbackBackend` (Ed25519+AES-256-GCM, docstring "not quantum-safe") when `import oqs` fails while the facade still advertises "Post-Quantum" crypto (no warning) — H152 family re-confirmed; 🔴 **H217** `AuthManager.save_to_file` persists provider credentials as **plaintext JSON** with no `0600`/keyring/encryption (secret-at-rest); 🔴 **H221** `quantum_server.py` binds **`0.0.0.0:8420`** with **wildcard CORS `allow_origins=["*"]` + `allow_credentials=True`** and **no authn/authz on any endpoint** (unauthenticated network surface, H184/H198/H205 family). 🟡 **H218** `cloud/auth._http_request` provider OAuth2/REST with no TLS cert pinning; 🟡 **H219** `qkd.key_reconciliation` is a toy that flips Bob's bits to match Alice (defeats QKD error reconciliation, forces QBER≈0); 🟡 **H220** `qrng`/`QuantumEntropy` are simulators deriving bytes from a seedable statevector, NOT a true entropy source (security callers get deterministic PRNG). 💭 **H222** CLI `--token` passed as plaintext argv (visible in `ps`/history); 💭 **H223** second Apache-2.0 header stray (`quantum_sim.py:20`, H7); 💭 **H224** `IBMBackend.__init__` `print(f"IBM Backend init warning: {e}")` can leak token/URL text (backend.py should follow providers/base redaction); 💭 **H225** provider REST tokens (IBM `Bearer`/IonQ `apiKey`/AWS `AWS4-HMAC-SHA256`/Rigetti `Bearer`) sent in clear, no TLS pinning (overlaps H218). **Fix order**: enforce GPL-3.0 headers (H7) → wire real PQC or fail CLOSED on missing liboqs (no silent classical downgrade) → encrypt/0600 credential store + keyring → gate `quantum_server` behind authn + restrict CORS + bind localhost → TLS pinning for provider auth → fix `key_reconciliation` + relabel `qrng` as simulator.
- [ ] **[UmerOS] `root/` study notes (v1.33).** `root/` = **9 FHS `/root` admin-home modules (~87 KB)** (`__init__`, `__main__`, `home`, `dotfiles`, `shell`, `mail`, `safety`, `passwd`, `fhs`) + `README.md` — pure-Python FHS /root home-manager (resolve root's home from /etc/passwd UID 0, bootstrap the dir @0700, materialise hardened dotfiles, manage ~/.forward, audit PATH/LD_*/.ssh/history). **Among the cleanest folders in the tree for dangerous-call hygiene**: grep across all 9 files found ZERO `subprocess`/`eval`/`exec`/`shell=True`/`os.system`/`pickle`/`ctypes`/`yaml.load`/`__import__`/`tarfile`/`shutil.rmtree`; every `os.chmod` uses safe modes (0700/0644/0600), `shell.py` strips `LD_PRELOAD`/`LD_AUDIT`/`GCONV_PATH` and `shlex.quote`s its exports, and `safety.py` is a model read-only auditor (severity-ranked findings). **But it carries the same cross-cutting gaps as the rest of the tree**: 🟡 **H226** 10 Apache-2.0 strays (9 `.py` + README 'GPL-3.0 (GNU General Public License Version 3)') — the single largest single-folder cluster, reinforcing H7; 🟡 **H227** `passwd.PasswdManager.write()`/`CanonicalRootBuilder.upsert()` rewrite `/etc/passwd` with **no `CapabilityManager` gate and no root-uid check** (cap-gate family H27/H28/H56/H92/H110/H156/H166/H205/H206); 🟡 **H228** `mail.RootMailForwarder.ensure()` creates the home dir with default umask perms (~0755) instead of 0700 — only `.forward` is tightened to 0600, leaving the parent world-readable. 💭 **H229** `passwd.write()` copies the `.bak` with default umask (potentially world-readable); 💭 **H230** `home.RootHomeManager.ensure()` `os.chmod` follows symlinks + the CLI `ensure [path]` takes an arbitrary path (symlink-target perms could change); 💭 **H231** `dotfiles.register_template(name, …)` → `ensure()` writes `home/name`, and a name with `/`/`..` escapes the home. **Models to copy**: `shell.RootShellEnvironmentBuilder` (strip DANGEROUS_VARS + shlex.quote), `safety.RootSafetyAuditor` (read-only, severity-ranked). **Fix order**: GPL headers (H7) → capability-gate the `/etc/passwd` write (root/uid-0 check) → tighten `mail.ensure()` home perms to 0700 → 0600 the `.bak` → symlink/realpath guard on the `ensure` path → validate dotfile template names.

- [ ] **[UmerOS] `sbin/` study notes (v1.34).** `sbin/` = **9 FHS `/sbin` system-binary modules (~95 KB)** (`__init__`, `sbin_manager`, `boot`, `filesystem`, `modules`, `network`, `system`, `mount`, `maintenance`) - a **model/registry package of FHS `/sbin` privileged commands** (halt/poweroff/reboot/init/shutdown, mount/umount/mknod/losetup/pivot_root, fdisk/fsck/mkfs/swapon/chroot, insmod/lsmod/modprobe/rmmod/depmod, ifconfig/ip/route, sysctl/hwclock/ldconfig, tune2fs/e2fsck/mke2fs/ctrlaltdel/kbdrate/loadkeys/dump/restore/sln/mktemp/setfdprm/rdev). **Cleanest folder in the tree for both dangerous-call hygiene AND zero-trust posture**: grep across all 9 files found **ZERO** `subprocess`/`eval`/`exec`/`shell=True`/`os.system`/`pickle`/`ctypes`/`yaml.load`/`__import__`/`tarfile`/`shutil.rmtree`/`os.mknod`/`os.chmod`/`CapabilityManager`, and every command is **fully simulated** (prints + returns 0/1, mutating in-memory dicts; no real privileged syscall). Only `maintenance.MktempCommand` performs a real FS write - via `tempfile.mkstemp/mkdtemp` (safe 0600, atomic), the one genuinely safe real side-effect. **Remaining gaps are license + aspirational architecture, no live exploits**: 🟡 **H232** **missing GPL-3.0 header in all 9 modules** (zero `GPL`/`Apache`/`Copyright` strings found - the "missing-header" variant of H7, distinct from the Apache-2.0 strays elsewhere; this package silently ships no license at all). 💭 **H233** `SbinManager.execute()` (`sbin_manager.py`) + each command `execute()` run privileged-simulated ops with **no `CapabilityManager` gate and no audit logging** - sets an ungated pattern for when real syscalls are wired; 💭 **H234** `mount.py`/`filesystem.py` privileged ops are simulated against in-memory tables (`_MOUNT_TABLE`/`_DEVICE_NODES`/`_LOOP_DEVICES`/`_SUPERBLOCKS`) - when wired to real `mount`/`os.mknod`/`chroot` they MUST enforce cap-gating + `nosuid,nodev,noexec` (per mnt/media H156-H168) + path validation; 💭 **H235** commands **masquerade as functional privileged tools** (`halt`/`mount -a`/`shutdown` print + return 0 but do nothing) - risk of false state/deception if treated as authoritative. **Models to copy**: `mount.py` in-memory mount table + global-state save/restore in `_selftest()` (good test hygiene); `maintenance.MktempCommand` safe `tempfile` usage. **Fix order**: add GPL-3.0 headers (H7) → when real syscalls are wired, capability-gate every privileged op + audit-log invocations → enforce mount hardening + path validation → clearly label simulated commands.

- [ ] **[UmerOS] `scripts/` study notes (v1.35).** `scripts/` = **2 tooling scripts (1,736 bytes)** (`install_deps.py`, `test_endpoint.py`) - a tiny misc-helpers dir (NOT a shipped runtime package; dev/CI utilities). Both use `subprocess` **injection-safe** (argv-list, no `shell=True`) - the one hygiene bright spot. **But both are unlicensed and the test harness normalizes a known 🔴**: 🟡 **H236** no GPL-3.0 header in either file (missing-header variant of H7, like sbin H232); 🟡 **H237** `test_endpoint.py:18` `generate_test_jwt()` hardcodes `secret = "test-secret"` (HS256) that is the **same** trusted secret as `drivers.driver_service` (🔴 H64) - the test legitimates the broken auth and would PASS against the vulnerable service, masking H64; 💭 **H238** `test_endpoint.py` uses a fixed `time.sleep(3)` to await server readiness instead of a health/readiness probe (flaky on slow CI) and hits the endpoint with a forged token bypassing any `CapabilityManager`/auth the real app would enforce; 💭 **H239** `install_deps.py:6` `pip install -r requirements.txt` with no pinning/integrity verification (supply-chain: a compromised/edited `requirements.txt` installs arbitrary packages as the current user) + `capture_output=True` buffers the whole install log; neither file has `from __future__` or a `_selftest()`. **Models to copy**: argv-list `subprocess` (no `shell=True`) in both. **Fix order**: add GPL headers (H7) → rotate the `drivers.driver_service` secret + make `test_endpoint` use a per-run ephemeral secret / skip auth in test mode (don't share `test-secret` with prod, H64) → poll a readiness endpoint instead of `sleep(3)` → pin/verify `requirements.txt` hashes.

- [ ] **[UmerOS] `sdk/` study notes (v1.36).** `sdk/` = **3 modules (~13 KB)** (`__init__`, `app_template`, `build_tool`) - a **developer SDK**: `UmerApp` app base class (lifecycle hooks + `read_file`/`write_file`/`send_ipc`/`ask_ai` delegated to a bound `kernel_api`) + `BuildTool` scaffolder/packager + a `HelloWorldApp` example. **Clean dangerous-call hygiene AND a good zero-trust delegation pattern**: grep found ZERO `subprocess`/`eval`/`exec`/`os.system`/`pickle`/`ctypes`/`__import__`/`tarfile`/`shutil.rmtree`/`os.mknod`/`os.chmod`/`CapabilityManager`; every FS/IPC/AI op goes through `self._kernel_api` behind `hasattr` guards that raise `RuntimeError("Kernel API not bound.")` if unbound (the SDK never touches the FS directly - a model for other packages). **Remaining gaps are license + input-validation**: 🟡 **H240** missing GPL-3.0 header in all 3 files (H7 missing-header variant, like sbin H232 / scripts H236); 🟡 **H241** `build_tool.scaffold`/`package` interpolate unvalidated `app_name` into a VFS path (`/sdk/projects/{app_name}/...`, L25/71) - a name with `/`/`..` escapes `/sdk/projects/`, so the SDK performs an unvalidated path build (VFS-scoped, lower blast radius than a real FS, but no normalization); 💭 **H242** `scaffold(custom_app_code=…)` writes caller-supplied arbitrary code verbatim into `src/main.py` via `vfs.write_file` (untrusted code templating, caller-cooperation assumed; becomes arbitrary code placement if the VFS is ever real-FS-backed); 💭 **H243** no `from __future__ import annotations` in any module, `__init__.py` is a bare re-export with no `__all__`, and there is no `_selftest()`/test. **Models to copy**: `app_template.UmerApp` kernel-API delegation with `hasattr` + `RuntimeError` guard (zero-trust access, never direct FS). **Fix order**: add GPL headers (H7) → validate/normalize `app_name` (reject `/`/`..`, resolve under `/sdk/projects`) → sanitize/limit `custom_app_code` (or document it as trusted-developer input) → add `__all__` + `_selftest()`.
- [ ] **[UmerOS] `security/` study notes (v1.37).** `security/` = **14 modules (~127 KB)** -- the OS security subsystem: 6 top-level (`__init__`, `crypto_engine`, `firewall`, `sandbox`, `security`, `tls_utils`) + an `antivirus/` subpackage (8 modules: `engine`, `scanner`, `signatures`, `heuristics`, `quarantine`, `realtime`, `api_server`, `__init__`). **Mixed folder -- partially real, partially aspirational/misleading**: real crypto (AES-256-GCM via `cryptography`, requires the lib, no insecure fallback), real hardened TLS (`create_ssl_context` CERT_REQUIRED + hostname check + TLS1.2 floor), real hashing/signatures (SHA3 + `hmac.compare_digest`), real AV mechanics (file hashing, regex heuristics, quarantine move). **But it ships the same cross-cutting gaps plus security-specific false claims**: 🔴 **H244** `security.SecureBoot` `strict_mode=False` default -> unknown components **allowed unverified** (fail-open; docstring claims every image hash-verified but the default dev path skips verification; the hash logic itself is real SHA3-256 + compare_digest); 🔴 **H245** `antivirus/api_server.py` aiohttp AV server on `127.0.0.1:9095` with **NO authn/authz** -- endpoints include `POST /api/quarantine` (file deletion via quarantine), `/api/quarantine/delete`, `/api/scan/directory` (scan arbitrary path), `/api/realtime/watch` (watch arbitrary dir) -- any local process can delete/quarantine files with no capability gate (zero-trust violation, H64/H221 family, localhost-scoped); 🔴 **H246** `sandbox.SecuritySandbox` provides **no real isolation** -- in-memory dict of processes/permissions, default `fs_root="/"`, no namespaces/seccomp/chroot, `check_permission`/`grant_permission` advisory only, NOT wired to any `CapabilityManager` despite docstring (masquerades as zero-trust, H110/H235 family); `resolve_path` uses correct realpath+commonpath but nothing enforces callers use it. 🟡 **H247** `security.IPCAuthenticator` docstring/module claim Anti-Replay Protection but live `verify_message` has **no nonce/timestamp check** (replay-protected version commented out) -- replay possible, misleading claim (H136/H139); 🟡 **H248** `security.AIBehavioralMonitor` AI-driven is a 4-entry static rule set, `_trigger_quarantine` only logs (no isolation) -- decorative AI + cosmetic quarantine, flags normal behaviour (false positives); 🟡 **H249** `sandbox.verify_signature` labeled code signing but is a hash-equality check (SHA3-512 of payload vs string), no key -- mislabeled; 🟡 **H250** `firewall.AIFirewall` AI-driven firewall is a toy that only blocks `dst_port==22`, no real inspection/state/egress filter, nothing enforces -- decorative/masquerading + merged from Deepseek code (unclear provenance); 🟡 **H251** `antivirus/quarantine.py` `restore_file` trusts stored `original_path` (absolute) + `os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(original_path), exist_ok=True)` -> arbitrary-file-write/path-traversal on restore if `quarantine.json` tampered (dir/metadata world-readable by default), and `quarantine_file` **irreversibly deletes** the original (`os.remove`); 🟡 **H252** `antivirus/heuristics.py` `analyze` reads **whole file into memory** (`f.read()`, no size cap) -> memory-exhaustion DoS, plus Windows/PE-centric patterns give poor Linux (ELF) coverage + false positives (`reverse_tcp <ip>`); 🟡 **H253** `antivirus/signatures.py` builtin DB uses **fake/placeholder hashes** (Zeus sha256 9b86c3b, CryptoMiner md5 a1b2c3d4) -> detects essentially no real malware (only EICAR real), `_load_custom_signatures` silently swallows `JSONDecodeError`/`KeyError` (`pass`) -> fail-open on malformed DB; 🟡 **H254** **missing GPL-3.0 header in all 14 modules** (zero license strings; H7 missing-header cluster, like sbin H232 / scripts H236 / sdk H240; only `security.py`+`tls_utils.py` have `from __future__`, the other 12 do not). 💭 **H255** `antivirus/realtime.py` real-time monitor is **polling** (`os.walk`+mtime every 2s), not FS-event-driven -> 2s blind window + CPU on large trees, `asyncio.get_event_loop()` deprecated; 💭 **H256** dead code -- `security.py` (~700 lines) + `sandbox.py` (~200 lines) contain large commented-out alternative implementations, `quarantine.py` imports unused `shutil`/`base64`, no `_selftest()`/test anywhere in the folder; 💭 **H257** `tls_utils.create_ssl_context` docstring claims TLS 1.3-first but default `min_version=TLSv1_2`, `run_uvicorn_secure` defaults `host="0.0.0.0"` (broad bind, TLS-terminated); 💭 **H258** `crypto_engine` `sign`/`verify` are HMAC-SHA512 Dilithium placeholder (honestly labeled, not PQC) + master key **ephemeral** (`os.urandom(32)` per instance, not persisted) -> sigs/decrypt do not survive restart and two instances cannot cross-verify + `print()` on init instead of logging. **Models to copy**: `tls_utils.create_ssl_context` (hardened TLS, strict default), `security.SecureBoot._compute_file_hash` (real SHA3-256 streaming + compare_digest), `antivirus/scanner.py` real file hashing + signature lookup. **Fix order**: make `SecureBoot` strict by default (fail CLOSED) -> authn + capability-gate the AV API (or unix socket) -> wire `SecuritySandbox` to real `CapabilityManager` (or relabel simulated) -> implement IPC replay protection (nonce+timestamp) -> replace decorative AI/firewall with real or label -> validate quarantine restore path (realpath under quarantine, never trust stored original_path) + do not delete original on quarantine -> cap heuristic file size + broaden coverage -> ship real signatures or label demo -> GPL headers (H7) + `from __future__` + remove dead code.

Tuple`. **Fix order**: add `filter="data"` to both extractall (H265/H266) -> capability-gate the rmtree calls (H267/H268) -> GPL-3.0 headers (H7 H269, H275) -> decouple paths from __init__ + runtime/arg resolve Windows paths (H270) -> relative imports + drop sys.path hacks (H271) -> validate/escape config inputs (H272) -> cap-gate chmod when real-wired (H273) -> drop dead imports (H276/H277) -> localhost+auth the test server (H274).

💭 **H287** global singleton `get_default_tmp_manager` triggers `bootstrap()` mkdir/chmod as a hidden init side effect. **Fix order**: GPL headers (H7 H278, H284) -> runtime/arg resolve tmp root + explicit bootstrap (H279/H287) -> relative imports + drop sys.path hacks (H280) -> cap-gate reaper + assert `tmp_root` under real `/tmp` (H281) -> validate/normalize tmpfs node names (H282) -> cap-gate `enforce_permissions` (H283) -> drop dead imports (H285) -> keep narrow ctypes shim documented (H286).

💭 **H294** `installer.py:12,17` `logging.info` used with **no `logging.basicConfig`/handler** -- all log records are dropped (observability gap); 💭 **H295** `installer.py` no module docstring/`__all__`/type hints on `install_umer_os(target_dir: str = ...)` (per-file baseline gap). **Fix order**: GPL-3.0 header + `from __future__` (H7 H291) -> capability-gate the rmtree + require explicit opt-in (H288) -> runtime/arg-resolve `target_dir` (no hardcoded `/umer_os`, H289) -> resolve `src` from script dir/explicit root (H290) -> surface missing-source errors, don't fail open (H293) -> configure logging (H294) -> non-interactive EULA flag + GPL-aligned text (H292/H295).

`games_data:120-121`; `dconf:73-75`; `icc_profile:111-112`; `nls:86`; `ppd:110`; `tmac:106`; `user_help:127`; `terminfo:111`; `wayland:102-103`; `xdg:73-74`; `xml:93`) -- hidden side effects, raise off non-root Linux, wrong-place trees off Linux; 🟡 **H298** fail-open broad `except Exception: return False` around privileged ops (`sendmail_manager`, `misc_data_manager`, `bsd_compat:220,235`, `games_data:229,244`, `xml:213,228`) -- security-relevant failures silently swallowed; 🟡 **H299** **H7 missing GPL-3.0 header cluster** -- only 3/61 (`kernel_source_manager`, `rpm_manager`, `source_manager`) carry any licence/Copyright/SPDX string, **58/61 ship no licence at all** (largest missing-header cluster in the tree); 🟡 **H300** hardcoded absolute `/usr/...` system paths baked into class constants (not runtime/arg-resolved), combined with import-time `mkdir` = non-portable + wrong-place creation. 💭 **H301** SIMULATED privileged/kernel-surface modules masquerade as functional -- `lib_manager`/`sbin_manager`/`rpm_manager`/`gcc_manager`/`x11_manager` (privileged FHS managers, in-memory only) + kernel-interface modules (`io_uring`,`landlock`,`seccomp`,`ntsync`,`iommufd`,`dmabuf`,`vduse`,`tee`,`netlink`,`procfs`,`sysfs`,`syscalls`) define constants/enums but have NO real syscall wiring (no `io_uring_setup`/`landlock_create_ruleset`/etc.) -- they imply OS access that doesn't exist (false-state risk, like sbin H235); 💭 **H302** per-file baseline gaps -- many modules lack `from __future__ import annotations`/module docstring/`__all__`/type hints, use lazy `import shutil` inside functions, and `usr/` ships **no `_selftest()`/test module at all**. **Fix order**: GPL headers on 58 modules (H7 H299) -> capability-gate LIVE privileged ops (H296) + move `mkdir` out of `__init__` behind explicit bootstrap (H297) -> runtime/arg-resolve `/usr` roots (H300) -> surface/avoid fail-open `except` (H298) -> label simulated managers + wire or clearly mark kernel-surface modules (H301) -> add baseline + `_selftest` (H302).

Dangerous-call surface clean (ZERO `subprocess`/`eval`/`exec`/`tarfile`/`zipfile`/`ctypes`/`sys.path`); the only destructive call is `shutil.rmtree`x3, but all real FS writes are fed by **unsanitized path params** (the headline risk). NO `CapabilityManager` gate anywhere; NO construction-time `mkdir` (on-demand, scoped -- better than `usr/` H297). **Findings: 1 🔴 + 3 🟡 + 1 💭**. 🔴 **H303** **path-traversal across all three Var managers** -- `name`/`username`/`directory`/`filename` are joined as `self.<x>_path / param` with no scoping/`realpath` check; `Path` collapses `..`, so `remove_local_item("../../etc/passwd")`→`unlink("/etc/passwd")`, `VarDirectoryManager.remove_local_item("../etc")`→`shutil.rmtree("/etc")`, and `SpoolManager.set_cron_user("../../etc/cron.d/x", jobs)`→writes `/etc/cron.d/x` = **cron RCE as root**; `LogManager.write_log("../../etc/cron.d/x", …)` = arbitrary append (CWE-22). 🟡 **H304** privileged FS ops (write/append/unlink/rmtree/rename) with **no `CapabilityManager` gate** (cap-gate family H227/H233/H267/H273/H281/H283/H296); 🟡 **H305** fail-open broad `except Exception: return False/None` (bare `except Exception: pass` in `directory_manager.py:154`) swallows security-relevant failures; 🟡 **H306** H7 **Apache-2.0 strays** -- `directory_manager.py:14`/`spool_manager.py:13`/`log_manager.py:13` declare `Licence: GPL-3.0 (GNU General Public License Version 3)` (conflicts GPL-3.0 canonical; `__init__.py` has no header -- opposite of `usr/`'s missing-header H299). 💭 **H307** `var/` ships **no `_selftest()`/test module**; baseline otherwise good (`from __future__`, type hints, docstrings, logging) but uses lazy `import shutil`/`import gzip` inside functions. **Note:** `var/lib/umeros/consent_ledger.json` is a real persistence artifact (links to the `legal/` consent ledger, H128-H141) -- worth a cross-folder check that the ledger is written/read under a capability + not world-readable. **Fix order**: validate/normalize path params (reject `..`, `realpath` under the manager root) -- H303 → capability-gate all privileged FS ops (H304) → surface fail-open `except` (H305) → GPL-3.0 headers (H7 H306) → add `test_var.py` (H307).
### 4.5 Performance 🟡
- [ ] **No N+1 / repeated work** in loops (e.g. IPC send-in-loop, fs lookups).
- [ ] **Compression tests use compressible data** (`b"Umer OS test pattern " * N`), never `os.urandom()` — LZMA on random data times out (per dev guide).
- [ ] **Async correctness** — `await` coroutines; no blocking calls on the event loop; `sched.stop()` awaited in teardown; `asyncio.Lock` guards shared queues (per scheduler design).
- [ ] **No unbounded growth** — caches/maps bounded or sized.

### 4.6 Testing 🔴/🟡
- [ ] **Behavioral change has tests.** New command → add to its suite + a `_selftest()` entry; new kernel/security/quantum module → suite tests (see `tests/` mappings).
- [ ] **Tests are hermetic & fast** — no real network/host dependence unless explicitly a container/integration test; skip (don't hang) on missing platform (pattern from `liboqs/test_leaks.py`).
- [ ] **Assert the contract** — return codes, raises, HMAC verify, capability allow/deny, signature check — not just "runs without error."
- [ ] **No regressions** — full suite green (921) before merge.
- [ ] **Flaky-test check** — tests don't depend on wall-clock, ordering, or global state.

### 4.7 Documentation & process 💭/🟡
- [ ] **PR description** explains *what* and *why*; references an issue if applicable.
- [ ] **Public API/docstring updates** included; `docs/` regenerated if API changed.
- [ ] **[UmerOS] License consistency** — headers/comments don't contradict GPL-3.0 (canonical, per LICENSE/setup.py/README); fix the Apache-2.0 strays in `developer_guide.md` and master prompts (H7).
- [ ] **[UmerOS] UI-tech consistency** — Flutter (Dart) is canonical (decided 2026-08-20). Remove/refresh Kivy references from `README.md`/`setup.py`/`requirements.txt` as legacy; treat any new Kivy-only UI code as legacy (H11, H15, §1.1).
- [ ] **[UmerOS] README accuracy** — PRs that *match* README's documented structure are fine, but reviewers must not reject code because README's "Project Structure" or code examples disagree with the real repo (README is aspirational; see H14). When a PR changes public APIs, the matching README example/section should be updated or flagged in the PR.
- [ ] **[UmerOS] Contributing-tooling alignment** — README tells contributors to `pip install black flake8 mypy pylint` and run them, but those are commented out of `requirements.txt` and not enforced. Canonical tooling = **Ruff + Mypy** per §7; update README's "Contributing"/"Development Setup" to match (H9/H15).
- [ ] **Changelog / tier migration** noted when moving code `EXPERIMENTAL`→`TODAY`.

---

### 4.8 Frontend — Flutter/Dart & HCI principles 🟡

The frontend lives in **`ui/flutter_ui/`** and is written in **Dart** (Flutter). It must follow established **Human-Computer Interaction (HCI)** principles as a design mandate (the user explicitly requires HCI adherence). The current UI already does well: Material 3 theming (light/dark), `tooltip:` on icon buttons (a11y), keyboard shortcuts (Ctrl+K search, Esc to dismiss), a responsive windowing model (drag/resize/snap), notifications, and a spotlight search — good visibility, flexibility, and recognition-over-recall. Review new frontend PRs against these heuristics:

- [ ] **[HCI] Visibility of system status.** Show loading/working states; confirm async actions (OTA, package install, network calls) where they happen; surface errors in-context. (Nielsen #1)
- [ ] **[HCI] Match the real world / plain language.** Avoid OS-internal jargon in user-facing labels — e.g. "CPUIdle & Governor" / "Power & Idle" are developer terms; prefer "Power & Performance". (Nielsen #2)
- [ ] **[HCI] User control & freedom.** Every destructive or mode-changing action has an undo/escape path: window close/min/max are present; destructive ops (file delete, package remove) must confirm or be reversible. (Nielsen #3)
- [ ] **[HCI] Consistency & standards.** Use Material 3 components consistently; **do not duplicate the app registry** — `desktop_shell.dart` hardcodes the same app list in `_DesktopGrid`, `_GlobalSearchModal`, and `_LaunchPad` (DRY/consistency risk, Hotspot H23). Centralise it in one model. (Nielsen #4)
- [ ] **[HCI] Error prevention.** Confirm before destructive actions; validate inputs (e.g. the Control Center custom-wallpaper path field). (Nielsen #5)
- [ ] **[HCI] Recognition over recall.** Keep LaunchPad, Dock, and spotlight search; don't force GUI users to memorise commands. (Nielsen #6)
- [ ] **[HCI] Flexibility & efficiency.** Preserve keyboard shortcuts + draggable/resizable windows; expose accelerators for power users. (Nielsen #7)
- [ ] **[HCI] Aesthetic & minimalist.** M3 already applied; avoid clutter, keep one primary action per surface. (Nielsen #8)
- [ ] **[HCI] Help & documentation.** GUI surfaces self-explanatory; keep the About/Help entry and add first-run hints. (Nielsen #10)
- [ ] **[HCI] Accessibility (a11y).** Add `Semantics` labels for screen readers on custom widgets (dock, draggable windows, launchpad); ensure colour contrast in both themes; respect `uiScale`/`enableGlassmorphism`. Test with `find.bySemanticsLabel`, not just a mount smoke test (Hotspot H26). (Nielsen #9 + WCAG 2.1 AA)
- [ ] **[UmerOS] Frontend is Dart-only.** No Python/Kivy/Tkinter in the UX path. Legacy `ui/*.py` (Tkinter/CLI) is a retired headless fallback only (H25). Native Android platform code, if any, is Kotlin/Java (not the app logic).
- [ ] **[UmerOS] Frontend egress respects privacy.** The Dart UI uses `http` + `webview_flutter_windows` (browser/network apps) — any call sending user data off-device must pass the same opt-in consent gate as the backend (`AIGovernance`, §4.3 / H18).

### 4.9 Build & release integrity 🟡

`build/` is the **PyInstaller output** for the `UmerOS-GUI` freeze. Of its 16 files (~42 MB), only **2 are authored**: `build/UmerOS-GUI.spec` (PyInstaller spec) and `build/__init__.py` (empty package marker). Everything under `build/UmerOS-GUI/` — `*.toc`, `PYZ-00.pyz`, `UmerOS-GUI.pkg`, `base_library.zip`, `localpycs/*.pyc`, `warn-*.txt`, `xref-*.html` — is **generated** and correctly **out of review scope** per §0 (generated `.pyz`/`.exe` artifacts). The reviewable surface is the `.spec`. Build/release PRs must satisfy:

- [ ] **[UmerOS] Build targets the canonical frontend.** `build/UmerOS-GUI.spec:5` freezes `ui/umeros_gui.py` — the **legacy Tkinter GUI that H25 already retired** as superseded by Flutter (`ui/flutter_ui/`). Shipping this binary ships the wrong (superseded) frontend. Repoint the build at the canonical frontend before any release; don't distribute the retired Tkinter app as "UmerOS" (Hotspot H41).
- [ ] **[UmerOS] Frozen artifacts are signed.** `build/UmerOS-GUI.spec:36` sets `codesign_identity=None` — the distributable is **unsigned**, which breaks the zero-trust signed-artifact mandate (§4.2, signed `.umerpkg`, H17/H27/H28). Sign the frozen binary + `.pkg` before distribution; reconsider `upx=True` (L29), which packs/obscures the payload and trips AV (Hotspot H42).
- [ ] **[UmerOS] Build is reproducible / portable.** The spec hardcodes an absolute Windows path `F:\Pension Person Details\UmerOS\ui\umeros_gui.py` (L5) — machine-specific, breaks in CI/other dev machines. Derive the entry from the repo root so the build runs anywhere (Hotspot H43).
- [ ] **[UmerOS] Complete, optimized freeze.** `datas=[]`/`binaries=[]`/`hiddenimports=[]`/`excludes=[]` and `optimize=0` (L8-15) declare no assets/runtime deps and ship unoptimized bytecode — risk of missing data files / Tk runtime on a clean machine. Declare needed `datas`/`binaries`/`hiddenimports`, set `optimize>=1`, and verify the frozen app runs from a clean VM (Hotspot H44).
- [ ] **[UmerOS] Build is codified.** The build is invoked ad-hoc (`pyinstaller UmerOS-GUI.spec`); there is **no `Makefile`/`build.py`/CI build step** in-repo (extends H16 — no CI test step either). Codify the build + a signed-artifact release job so it is reproducible and auditable (Hotspot H44).
- [ ] **[UmerOS] Spec carries a license/tier label.** `build/UmerOS-GUI.spec` has no license header or `[TODAY]/[EXPERIMENTAL]/[FUTURE]` label (extends H7/H30/H40); `build/__init__.py` is a harmless 0-byte marker. Add a header to the spec for consistency (Hotspot H45).

---

## 5. The review process (workflow)

```
 Author dev → self-check (§4) → open PR → CI gates (§7) → ≥1 reviewer →
 resolve 🔴, waive 🟡 (maintainer) → approval → squash-merge to main →
 post-merge: confirm green, update docs
```

1. **Author self-check.** Run §4 against the diff locally. Run `ruff`, `bandit -c pyproject.toml`, `mypy`, and the relevant `tests/` suite.
2. **Open PR.** Target `main`. Fill the PR template (Section 10). Link issue. Keep it focused. Mark the PR's own tier.
3. **CI gates block merge** until green (lint, type, security baseline, full test suite, coverage floor).
4. **Assign reviewer(s).** At least one human reviewer; security-sensitive PRs (auth, IPC, crypto, `subprocess`, `eval`, self-healing/auto-patch, package signing, installer) require a **security reviewer** sign-off.
5. **Review round.** Use 🔴/🟡/💭 inline. One complete pass, not drip-fed. Ask when intent is unclear rather than assuming wrong.
6. **Address.** Author fixes 🔴, responds to each 🟡 (fix or justify). Re-request review.
7. **Approve & merge.** Maintainer merges (squash) only when §8 criteria are met.
8. **Post-merge.** Verify CI on `main` is green; update `docs/`/README test counts if they changed.

**SLA (suggested):** author responds within 2 business days; reviewer first pass within 2 business days.

---

## 6. Roles & responsibilities

| Role | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| **Author** | Small focused PR; passes §4 self-check & all CI; addresses every comment; keeps suite green |
| **Reviewer** | Domain + standards check; at least one 🔴/🟡 pass; approves only when comfortable owning the merge |
| **Security reviewer** | Sign-off on auth/IPC/crypto/sandbox/`subprocess`/`eval`/self-healing/package-signing/installer PRs; verifies no secret leakage |
| **Maintainer** | Enforces merge criteria (§8); waives 🟡 with rationale; protects `main`; curates baselines; resolves design inconsistencies (H7/H11) |

---

## 7. Automation & CI (build on what exists)

The repo already runs `.github/workflows/security_scan.yml` (Bandit, Safety, Trivy, ZAP). **Extend it; do not start from scratch.**

**Add to the pipeline (most are already in `requirements.txt` but commented out):**
- **Formatter/linter:** adopt **Ruff** (`ruff format` + `ruff check`) — fast, single tool. Add a `pyproject.toml` `[tool.ruff]` config. This replaces the commented `black`/`flake8` lines.
- **Type checker:** **Mypy** in non-strict (or gradual) mode; zero new `Any` in security-critical modules.
- **Static security:** keep **Bandit**, but scope it and baseline it:
  - Run `bandit -c pyproject.toml -r .` with `exclude` for `Old Linux Code/,node_modules/,*/__pycache__/,tests/` so the C reference tree stops creating noise.
  - Generate a baseline (`bandit -f json > bandit-baseline.json`) and fail only on *new* findings (use `bandit-baseline` diff) so we don't block on pre-existing debt.
- **Secret scanning:** add **gitleaks** (or `trufflehog`) as a pre-commit + CI step to catch the next `sk-or-v1-…` before it lands. Pre-commit should also run Ruff + a `.gitignore` guard.
- **Dependency scan:** keep **Safety**; add **pip-audit** as a cross-check. 🔴 **`g4f>=0.4.0`** (free GPT-4o/Claude via reverse-engineered endpoints, no API key) is a supply-chain **and** provider-ToS risk — do **not** rely on it for production paths; if used at all, vendor it, pin it, and isolate it behind a capability (Hotspot H15). **Pin everything**: replace floating `>=` with `==`/`~=` and add hashes; the unbounded `>` on `fastapi`/`uvicorn`/`httpx` can pull breaking majors. **Remove `kivy`** from `requirements.txt` (it is already commented out at line 52) **and from `setup.py` `install_requires` (line 15)** — Flutter (Dart) is now canonical (H11/§1.1), so Kivy is legacy drift to retire, not re-add (H15/H22).
- **Run the suite (currently missing — see H16):** add a CI step that executes the whole `tests/` tree. Standardize on **pytest** with a `pyproject.toml [tool.pytest]` config (`testpaths = tests`, `pythonpath = .`, `asyncio_mode = auto`) so it collects both the `unittest.TestCase` files and `tests/quantum/`. Pin `pytest>=8` (the bundled pytest imports the removed `imp` module and fails on Python 3.12+). With this, the "full suite green" gate in §8 becomes enforceable.
- **Coverage gate:** enforce a floor (e.g. new lines ≥ 80% covered) via `pytest-cov`; publish the report.
- **Pin actions** with commit SHAs (current `actions/checkout@v3` etc. are floating); the ZAP step targets `localhost:8000` — only include it when an API server actually runs in CI, or it's a no-op.
- **Pre-commit:** `.pre-commit-config.yaml` running Ruff + gitleaks + trailing-whitespace/EOF on every local commit.

> Keep CI fast: run lint+type+unit in parallel; reserve Trivy/ZAP for `main`/release, not every PR.

---

## 8. Merge criteria — Definition of Done

A PR may merge **only when ALL** are true:

- [ ] 🔴 items resolved; 🟡 either fixed or waived in writing by a maintainer.
- [ ] **Tier label present** on the PR and on new modules/features.
- [ ] CI green: Ruff clean, Mypy clean (new code), Bandit no *new* findings vs baseline, full suite (921) passes, coverage floor met.
- [ ] At least one approval; security PRs have security-reviewer approval.
- [ ] No secrets committed (gitleaks clean); `.gitignore` covers `settings.local.json` and `*secret*`.
- [ ] Tests included for behavioral change; docs/API updated if changed.
- [ ] PR description complete; linked issue closed/updated.

---

## 9. Known hotspots — enforce these going forward

Concrete issues found while studying the repo + design sources (2026-08-20). Treat each as a standing review gate until fixed:

| ID | Severity | Location | Issue | Action |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| H1 | 🔴 | `settings.local.json` | Live OpenRouter API key committed in plaintext | Revoke+rotate, `.gitignore`, purge history |
| H2 | 🔴 | `initrd/ai_helper.py:166` | `eval(line)` on user history (suppressed B307) | Parse or gate behind capability + signed history |
| H3 | 🔴 | `lib/security.py:72` | Hardcoded default `PASSWORD="password"` | Generate at runtime / require set on first boot |
| H4 | 🟡 | `bin/user_commands.py:169-185` | `su`-like `[sh,"-c",command]` as other user; `_exec_shell` is a stub returning 0 | Validate command source; implement or clearly mark EXPERIMENTAL |
| H5 | 🟡 | `bin/boolean_ops.py:411`, `etc/issue_motd.py` | `subprocess` to host with arg lists | Keep list-form, no `shell=True`; sandbox on Windows host |
| H6 | 🟡 | `core/command.py` vs `bin/*` | Base `execute(*args)->Any` contradicts `execute(args=None)->int` | Converge base class to the adopted convention |
| H7 | 🟡 | LICENSE/setup.py/README (GPL-3.0) vs `developer_guide.md` & master prompts (Apache 2.0) | License inconsistency: 3 sources say GPL-3.0, 2 say Apache-2.0 | **Decision: GPL-3.0 canonical.** Fix Apache-2.0 strays in `developer_guide.md` + master prompts so docs stop contradicting the license |
| H8 | 🟡 | `bin/` (~30+) | Broad `except Exception`/`except:` swallow errors | Catch specific, log, re-raise where needed |
| H9 | 🟡 | CI | Only `security_scan.yml` runs (Bandit/Safety/Trivy/ZAP); **no test execution**, no Ruff/Mypy/pre-commit/coverage/secret-scan; Bandit scans the whole repo incl. the C reference tree | Implement Section 7; add a test-run step (H16) |
| H10 | 💭 | `kernel/umer_kernel.py:1717` | Commented `exec(chat_code,…)` AI-exec feature | Track as issue; gate behind allowlist+capability before enabling |
| H11 | 🟡 | UI: prior Kivy (`setup.py`/README) vs **decided Flutter (Dart)** | UI tech was inconsistent (Kivy in code, Flutter in blueprint). **Decided 2026-08-20: Flutter (Dart) canonical**, overriding Kivy. Repo is Kivy-based today → legacy drift to migrate; "Flutter with java" corrected to Dart | **Decision: Flutter canonical.** Drop `kivy` from `requirements.txt`; refresh Kivy references as legacy (H15/§1.1). Native Android code (if any) is Kotlin/Java, not the app logic |
| H12 | 🔴 | `ai/` self-healing (design) | AI hot-patch path, if enabled, applies generated code | Require capability scope + sandbox + audit log + rollback test before auto-apply (see §4.2) |
| H13 | 🟡 | `security/`/package signing (design) | `.umerpkg` signing + chain-of-trust must hold | Review any change to signing/verify flow as security-critical |
| H14 | 🟡 | `README.md` (Project Structure, code examples, Project Statistics) | README documents a **different, aspirational** layout (`kernel/ipc.py`, `kernel/hal.py`, `quantum/simulator.py`, `security/crypto.py`, `fs/quantum_fs.py`, `ui/shell.py`…) that does **not** exist; its code examples `import` those phantom modules (`from kernel.scheduler import Scheduler`, `from kernel.ipc import IPCBroker`, `from quantum.simulator import QuantumCircuitSimulator`); stats claim ~3,500 LOC / 12 core modules vs actual ~735 active modules, and omit the real heavy dirs (`bin/`, `etc/`, `lib/`, `usr/`, `initrd/`, `drivers/` ~77 files) | Treat `README.md` as marketing/aspiration, **not** a review oracle. Don't block PRs because they diverge from README's structure. Open an issue to refresh README "actual vs documented"; add a CI doc-drift check if feasible |
| H15 | 🟡 | `requirements.txt` (dependencies) | (a) `g4f>=0.4.0` — free GPT-4o via reverse-engineered endpoints, supply-chain + ToS risk; (b) floating `>=` pins and unbounded `>` on `fastapi`/`uvicorn`/`httpx` → non-reproducible builds; (c) `kivy` commented out — now **drop it entirely** since Flutter (Dart) is the canonical UI (H11/§1.1); the Kivy-based UI is legacy drift; (d) `black`/`flake8`/`mypy`/`pylint` commented out though README's Contributing tells contributors to install/run them | Pin deps (`==`/`~=` + hashes); review/vendor/isolate `g4f`; **remove `kivy`** from `requirements.txt`; align README Contributing with enforced Ruff+Mypy (H9) |
| H16 | 🟡 | `tests/` (harness) | Framework split: top-level `test_*.py` + `run_*.py` use `unittest`; `tests/quantum/` uses **pytest** (`pytest.fixture`, bare `assert`). No root pytest config (`pytest.ini`/`pyproject`/`setup.cfg`/`tox.ini`). The 3 `run_*.py` scripts run only **4 of 39** modules; **no CI step runs the suite** (only `security_scan.yml` exists) → the §8 "full suite green" gate is unenforceable. Design source `umer_os_skills.json` prescribes pytest, but the bundled pytest imports the removed `imp` module (broken on 3.12+) | Pick ONE framework (recommend **pytest** — it collects `unittest.TestCase` too); add `pyproject.toml [tool.pytest]` + a CI `pytest` step; repin `pytest>=8`; make the suite discoverable & green in CI (H9) |
| H17 | 🟡→🔴 | `security/security.py` → `SecureBoot.verify_image` (also planned `security/secure_boot.py`) | Signature/trust verification is **fail-open**: `verify_image` returns `True` when there is no trust-store entry and when the file does not exist (`test_security.py` asserts this as "permissive during development"). For a zero-trust OS this effectively disables code-signing enforcement | Before any security-sensitive/production merge, make `verify_image` **fail-closed** (unknown/missing = reject). Keep an explicit dev-mode flag if needed, never the default (H12/H13) |
| H18 | 🔴 | `ai/umer_ai.py:LocalAIAssistant.query` (→ `OnlineProvider`) | The assistant delegates to `OnlineProvider` (POSTs the user prompt to an external API) **without** an `AIGovernance.check_consent(...)` gate. The module's own header guarantees *"NO user data leaves the device by default"* — a network egress with no explicit, logged consent violates the §4.3 opt-in mandate and the privacy guarantee | Add a consent check (`AIGovernance.check_consent("online_ai")`, logged) before any outbound `providers["online"].query()`; default-deny. Wire `AIGovernance` into `LocalAIAssistant.__init__` |
| H19 | 🟡 | `ai/assistant.py`, `ai/self_healing.py`, `ai/resource_predictor.py` | (a) `assistant.py`/`self_healing.py` violate the per-file baseline — no type hints, no docstrings, no `logging`, no tier label, use `print`; (b) duplicated AI stacks: `AIAssistant`≈`LocalAIAssistant`, `SelfHealingService`≈`SelfHealingEngine`, `ResourcePredictor`≈`AIResourceManager` (two EWMA predictors, `SPIKE_Z_THRESHOLD` 2.0 vs 2.5) | Consolidate into `umer_ai.py`; bring `assistant.py`/`self_healing.py` up to baseline or delete them; pick one predictor (`AIResourceManager`) |
| H20 | 🟡 | `ai/providers.py` (header), `ai/resource_predictor.py` (header) | Module docstrings declare `Licence: GPL-3.0 (GNU General Public License Version 3)` while the repo is **GPL-3.0** (LICENSE/setup.py/README). Extends H7 to name these `ai/` files | Correct the headers to GPL-3.0 (or remove the licence line); see H7 |
| H21 | 🟡→🔴 | `ai/umer_ai.py:SelfHealingEngine` + `ai/self_healing.py:SelfHealingService` | Self-healing generates patch *code strings*; FUTURE is LLM auto-apply. TODAY's stubs are comment-only (safe), but `SelfHealingService.mitigate` "restarts process in isolated state" with no sandbox/audit/rollback. Any auto-apply of generated code is a 🔴 per H12 | Keep `generate_patch` output non-executable until the H12 gate exists; gate `mitigate` behind capability + audit + rollback; never `exec`/`eval` a patch |
| H22 | 🟡 | `setup.py:15` (`kivy>=2.3.0` in `install_requires`) + `README.md` (≈7 Kivy refs: L146 "built with Kivy", L167/L748 "Kivy-Based Shell", L206 "kivy 2.3+", L262 "shell.py … (Kivy)", L362 "(Kivy Shell, GUI)", L855 Kivy link) | Kivy still declared/described as **the** UI even though **Flutter (Dart) is canonical** (decided 2026-08-20, H11/§1.1). `requirements.txt:52` already comments kivy out, but `setup.py` still ships it as a hard dependency — a real install-time contradiction | Drop `kivy>=2.3.0` from `setup.py` `install_requires`; refresh `README.md` to point the UI at `ui/flutter_ui/` and remove Kivy mentions (extends H11/H14/H15). Kivy is legacy drift |

| H24 | 🟡 | `ui/flutter_ui/lib/src/core/desktop_shell.dart:287` "CPUIdle & Governor", `:448` "Power & Idle"; `apps/power_governor_app.dart:104` "CPUIdle & Power Governor Framework" | User-facing labels use **OS-internal jargon** — violates HCI #2 (match the real world / plain language). "CPUIdle & Governor" / "Power & Idle" read as subsystem names, not user goals | Rename to plain language ("Power & Performance", "Power Governor"); audit all 18 `apps/` screen titles for jargon; prefer task-oriented labels |
| H25 | 🟡 | `ui/*.py` (`fluidic_ui.py` CLI shell, `umer_de.py` Tkinter DE, `theme.py`, `launch_gui.py`, `umeros_gui.py`) | Legacy **Python/Tkinter** UI is superseded by the Flutter/Dart frontend (`ui/flutter_ui/`). `fluidic_ui.py` lazy-imports `ui.umer_de.UmerDE` (Tkinter). Any *new* UI code here is legacy drift | Keep only as a last-resort headless/legacy fallback; flag new UI code in `ui/*.py` as legacy; plan migration/removal to `ui/flutter_ui/`. Backend stays Python; only the *UX* path moves to Dart (§1.1) |
| H26 | 🟡 | `ui/flutter_ui/lib/src/widgets/*` (Dock, DraggableWindow, LaunchPad, ControlCenter) + `test/widget_test.dart` | **a11y gaps:** custom widgets lack `Semantics` labels for screen readers; the only test is a mount smoke test (`expect(find.byType(UmerOSApp), findsOneWidget)` + `pump(1s)`) — no `find.bySemanticsLabel`/interaction/a11y coverage (Nielsen #9 + WCAG 2.1 AA, §4.8) | Add `Semantics` (label/hint) to custom widgets; broaden `widget_test.dart` to assert semantics + key interactions (drag window, open launchpad, Ctrl+K). Treat a11y as a frontend merge gate |
| H27 | 🟢 | `boot/bootloader.py:147` (`verify_kernel`) | Kernel integrity check returns `True` when the image is **missing** ("Permissive during prototype phase") and when `expected_hash is None` (skipped). For a zero-trust OS an unverified/missing kernel is trusted — same class as H17 (SecureBoot fail-open) | Make verification **fail-closed**: missing image or unmatched/unknown hash ⇒ reject (or require an explicit dev flag). Default-deny; never trust an unverified kernel | **FIXED (session 14):** `verify_kernel` returns `False` on missing file (`# [TODAY] H27`) and on `required=True` with no hash; dev-mode shortcut only when `required=False` and hash is deliberately skipped. Regression-covered by `tests/test_boot_init.py::TestVerifyKernelFailClosed`. |
| H28 | 🟢 | `boot/efi_system.py:524-533` (`is_binary_trusted`) | Secure Boot trust returns `True` when state is `DISABLED` or `SETUP_MODE` and never consults `dbx` (forbidden list). A forbidden/dbx-listed binary is still "trusted" in those states — same class as H17 | Fail-closed: unknown/missing/dbx-listed ⇒ reject; only `USER_MODE`/`DEPLOYED_MODE` with a non-revoked `db` entry ⇒ trusted. Consult `dbx` before trusting | **FIXED (session 14):** `is_binary_trusted` defaults `strict=True` (fail-closed); DISABLED/SETUP_MODE ⇒ untrusted; dbx always rejected regardless of strict. Covered by existing `tests/test_efi_system.py` H28 tests. |
| H29 | 🟢 | `boot/init.py:27` (`display_waiver`) | The EULA/"I AGREE" liability waiver is **auto-accepted** when stdin is not a TTY ("[Non-interactive mode: Auto-accepting waiver for tests]"), bypassing the §4.2 installer legal mandate (no silent accept). 🔴 if it reaches a real installer path | Never silently accept the waiver; gate behind explicit opt-in/capability, or require a signed `--accept-eula` flag. Extends the installer EULA mandate | **FIXED (session 14):** `display_waiver` no longer silently auto-accepts; non-TTY without explicit opt-in aborts (`sys.exit(1)`). Consent only via TTY-typed 'I AGREE' or `accept_eula=True`/`--accept-eula`. Covered by `tests/test_boot_init.py::TestDisplayWaiverFailClosed`. |
| H30 | 🟡 | `boot/__init__.py:71`, `__main__.py:29`, `boot_manager.py:21`, `bootloader.py:22`, `bzimage.py:52`, `cmdline.py:40`, `efi_stub.py:50`, `fhs.py:43`, `info.py:28`, `initrd_manager.py:17` | 10 `boot/` modules declare `Licence: GPL-3.0 (GNU General Public License Version 3)` headers — contradicts GPL-3.0 (LICENSE/setup.py/README). Extends H7/H20 to name `boot/` files | Correct headers to GPL-3.0 (or drop the licence line); see H7/H20 |
| H31 | 🟡 | `boot/` (19 of 20 modules) | Only `bootloader.py` carries a `[TODAY]` tier label; the other 19 modules (`kernel_image`, `grub_manager`, `systemd_boot`, `efi_system`, `boot_params`, `microcode`, `boot_splash`, `crash_kernel`, `bzimage`, `efi_stub`, `cmdline`, `info`, `fhs`, `initrd_manager`, `boot_manager`, `__init__`, `__main__`, `demo_boot`, `init`) have none — violates the mandatory tier-label rule (§4.4) | Add a `[TODAY]`/`[EXPERIMENTAL]`/`[FUTURE]` label to every `boot/` module |
| H32 | 🟡 | `boot/uefi_stub.c` (33 lines) + `boot/init.py:33` | The only C file is a `printf` placeholder with **no real UEFI binding and no `ctypes` bridge** from Python, yet `init.py:33` claims "Initializing UEFI stubs via ctypes (Simulated)". Per §1.1 C/ASM is permitted only for bootloader entry / HW interrupt table / context-switch stub behind a `ctypes` binding | Implement the ctypes bridge or label it clearly `[FUTURE]` aspirational; don't claim a ctypes simulation that doesn't exist (realism label, §4.4) |
| H33 | 🟡 | `boot/bootloader.py:153` (SHA3-256) vs `boot/efi_system.py:82` (SHA-256) | Inconsistent hashing: kernel verified with SHA3-256, EFI binaries with SHA-256, while the design mandate (§4.2) prescribes **SHA3-512** | Standardize on SHA3-512 (or at least SHA3-256) across `boot/`; align with the zero-trust crypto standard |
| H34 | 🟡 | `boot/__main__.py` (CLI) + `boot/demo_boot.py` (imports) + `boot/init.py` (baseline) | `python -m boot` is a hand-rolled CLI that doesn't follow the `core/command.py` `execute(args=None)->int` contract; `demo_boot.py` uses unprefixed sibling imports (`from boot_params import`) that only work when run from inside `boot/`; `init.py` skips the per-file baseline (no `from __future__`, logging, docstrings) | Wrap `boot/` CLI as proper `core/command` commands (or document it as a standalone tool); use package-relative imports in `demo_boot.py`; bring `init.py` up to the baseline |
| H35 | 🟡 | `bin/` (27 of 44 command modules) | Command-interface contract drift: `def execute(self, *args)` instead of the adopted `def execute(self, args=None) -> int` (POSIX exit code). Violates H6 **at scale** — 190 method-level occurrences across `bash.py`, `usr_doc.py`, `usr_etc.py`, `usr_games.py`, `usr_include.py`, `usr_lib*.py`, `usr_libexec.py`, `usr_local*.py`, `usr_man.py`, `usr_sbin_cmds.py`, `usr_share*.py` (color/dict/doc/games/info/nls/locale/ppd/sgml/templates/xml), `usr_src.py`, `usr_tmp.py`, `usr_x11r6.py`. Only 12 modules (`user_commands.py`, `usr_cmds.py`, `archive.py`, `boolean_ops.py`, `csh.py`, `device.py`, `ed.py`, `home.py`, `network_cmds.py`, `process.py`, `shell.py`) use the correct signature | Converge all `bin/` commands to `execute(args=None)->int`; converge the `core/command.py` base class (H6) so new code inherits the right signature; this is the dominant H6 offender in the repo |

| H270 | 🟡 | `srv/fhs.py:51`, `srv/backup.py:37`, `srv/manager.py:49,57-71` | **Hardcoded Windows dev paths + `__init__`-time mkdir side effects** - `DEFAULT_SRV_ROOT = Path("F:/Pension Person Details/UmerOS/srv")`, `DEFAULT_BACKUP_DIR = Path("F:/Pension Person Details/UmerOS/var/backups/srv")`, and `DEFAULT_REGISTRY_PATH = Path("F:/Pension Person Details/UmerOS/srv/registry.json")` are hardcoded to one workstation (Windows-only `os.name=="nt"` branch); `SrvHierarchy.__init__` -> `_ensure_root()` does `mkdir(parents=True)`, `SrvBackupManager.__init__` makes `.backups`, and `SrvManager.__init__` constructs both + `load_registry()` + `auto_discover()` - so merely importing/building managers mutates the filesystem on the wrong path on other machines and raises on non-root Linux `/srv`. | Resolve root/backup/registry from env var/arg at runtime, avoid FS mutation in `__init__`, and move bootstrap/scan behind explicit `bootstrap()`/`scan()` calls. |
| H271 | 🟡 | `srv/*.py` (all modules) + `__init__.py:30-32`, `test_srv.py:16-21` | **Fragile absolute intra-package imports via sys.path hack** - modules import siblings by bare absolute name (`from fhs import ...`) instead of relative (`from .fhs import ...`); `__init__.py` and `test_srv.py` insert their own dir at `sys.path[0]` so these resolve, polluting global sys.path and colliding with any other top-level `fhs`/`service`/`permissions`/... (H261 family). | Convert intra-package imports to relative and delete the `sys.path.insert` hacks so `import srv` works as a proper subpackage. |
| H272 | 🟡 | `srv/protocols.py:211-226` `generate_nfs_export_line`/`generate_samba_share_section` | **Unvalidated interpolation into `/etc/exports` / `smb.conf` (config-injection)** - the functions build `f"{client}({options})"` and `[{share_name}]\n  path=...\n  comment={comment}` from caller strings with no validation/escaping, so a malicious `client`/`options`/`share_name`/`comment` injects extra NFS exports / Samba directives (e.g. `client="(rw,no_root_squash)"` or a newline in `comment` adding a new share). | Validate `client` against an IP/CIDR allowlist, quote/escape `comment` and `share_name`, and reject `options` not on a strict allowlist. |
| H273 | 🟡 | `srv/permissions.py:139-171` `apply_profile` (+ `audit_service` 173-225) | **POSIX perm mutation/audit with no `CapabilityManager` gate** - `apply_profile` performs real `os.chmod` (POSIX-gated to `os.name=="posix"`) to set per-protocol modes (incl. sticky bits); the op is advisory (no capability check) and `audit_service` walks trees flagging world-writable/no-sticky and world-readable-secret paths. On real wiring this must be a privileged, capability-gated action (cap-gate family H227/H233). | Gate `os.chmod` behind a `CapabilityManager` capability and a root-uid check when real-wired; keep `audit_service` read-only. |
| H274 | 💭 | `srv/protocols.py:64-78` `WWWServiceHandler.start_test_server` | **Localhost test HTTP server, no auth** - binds `socketserver.TCPServer(("127.0.0.1", port), ...)` (good, loopback-only) but serves an arbitrary `doc_root` with no authentication; a developer convenience, low risk, but it will serve any file under `doc_root` to local clients. | Add an explicit opt-in flag, keep loopback bind, and warn that it serves files unauthenticated. |
| H275 | 💭 | `srv/test_srv.py` | **Missing GPL-3.0 header (H7 missing-header variant)** - grep found ZERO `GPL`/`Apache`/`Copyright`/`Licence`/`License`/`SPDX` strings AND no `from __future__ import annotations`; the only module in the folder with neither a licence nor a future import (like sbin H232 / scripts H236 / sdk H240 / sources H260). | Add the canonical GPL-3.0 header and `from __future__ import annotations` to test_srv.py. |
| H276 | 💭 | `srv/service.py:15` | **Unused `import json`** - `import json` is declared but never referenced in the module (serialisation is absent here); dead import. | Remove the unused `import json`. |

| H279 | 🟡 | `tmp/fhs.py:50`, `tmp/hierarchy.py:47-58,80-96`, `tmp/manager.py:42-51` | **Hardcoded Windows dev path + `__init__`-time mkdir side effects** - `DEFAULT_TMP_ROOT = Path("F:/Pension Person Details/UmerOS/tmp")` when `os.name=="nt"` is hardcoded to one workstation (better than `srv/`'s 3 spots: centralized in `fhs.py`, siblings import it); `TmpHierarchy.__init__` -> `_ensure_root()` does `mkdir(parents=True)` + `os.chmod(..., 0o1777)`, `TmpManager.__init__` -> `_ensure_initialized()` -> `bootstrap()` (mkdirs), and `get_default_tmp_manager()` triggers bootstrap on first call - so merely importing/building a manager mutates the filesystem on the wrong path on other machines and raises on non-root Linux `/tmp`. | Resolve the tmp root from env var/arg at runtime, avoid FS mutation in `__init__`, and move bootstrap behind an explicit call. |

| H281 | 🟡 | `tmp/reaper.py:86-202` `clean_by_age`/`clean_on_boot`/`clean_by_quota` | **Destructive reaper with no capability gate and no `tmp_root` containment** - the reaper `unlink()`s/`rmdir()`s files by age/quota (its legitimate tmpwatch/systemd-tmpfiles job) and correctly excludes `PROTECTED_SOCKET_DIRS`, but `tmp_root` is any caller-supplied path with **no validation that it is the real `/tmp`** and **no `CapabilityManager` gate** (cap-gate family H227/H233); a misconfigured/attacker-influenced `tmp_root` would reap arbitrary files by age (milder than `srv/`'s whole-tree `rmtree` 🔴 because it is bounded by age/quota + excludes protected dirs, hence 🟡). | Require a `CapabilityManager` capability to run the reaper AND assert `tmp_root` resolves under the system `/tmp` (or an explicitly allowed location) before any deletion. |
